1.
Solid algebra and tubular neighbourhoods

In this section, we review some basic constructions about solid modules, following
[
3
]
, and introduce the recollement of the category of solid
modules associated to the tubular neighbourhood of a closed subscheme of an affine scheme and its complement.

1.1.
Generalities on solid modules

Consider a ring
A
of finite type over
\mathbb{Z}
. The category of discrete
A
-modules
(i.e. the category of
A
-modules in the stable symmetric monoidal category of usual
\mathbb{Z}
-module) we denote by
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\text{discr}}
.
Clausen and Scholze (
[
3
]
, Lectures 5,6 and 8) have defined the category
\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
of solid

A
-modules. In case
A=\mathbb{Z}
this category is called the category of solid abelian groups (see Lectures 5 and 6 in
[
3
]
)
and serves as our base cateogory in this paper.

\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
admits all limits and colimits and a continuous tensor product, denoted
\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
, along with a fully-faithful embedding

\iota\colon\mathrm{Mod}^{\text{discr}}_{A}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\;,

which is continuous and symmetric monoidal, but in general does not preserve general infinite limits, in particular does not preserve infinite products. The category
\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
is compactly
generated by objects of the form
\prod_{I}A
, where
A=\iota(A)
is the monoidal unit, the product is computed inside
\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
and is not necessarily
finite. On the compact generators, the tensor product and the right adjoint local hom are computed by the following formulas:

\begin{split}&\;\prod_{I}A\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\prod_{J}A\;\simeq\;\prod_{I\times J}A\;;\\
&\;\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\prod_{I}A,\prod_{J}A)\;\simeq\;\prod_{J}\bigoplus_{I}A\;.\end{split}

1.1.1.
Synthetic description of
\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}

Let
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\text{free, fin-len}}
denote the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Mod}^{\text{discr}}_{A}
spanned by finite complexes of free
A
-modules of not necessarily finite rank;
the functor

\textstyle{(\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\text{free, fin-len}})^{\mathrm{op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\in$}}}
\textstyle{\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\iota(F),A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\in$}}}

induces a fully faithful symmetric monoidal embedding

(1.1.1.1)

\bigl(\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\text{free, fin-len}}\bigr)^{\mathrm{op}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\;,

which factors over the subcategory
\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}^{\omega}\subset\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
of compact
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
-modules. Indeed, note that the functor sends
\oplus A\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\text{free, fin-len}}
to the product
\prod A\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
, and
the solid tensor product distributes such products.
We will need the following characterization of the subcategory
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\text{free, fin-len}}\subseteq\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
, which is a generalization of Lazard’s theorem,

[
10
,
Theorem 058G
]
.

Proposition 1.1.2
.

Let
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\text{Tor-fin}}\subseteq\mathrm{Mod}^{\text{discr}}_{A}
denote the subcategory spanned by complexes of bounded Tor-amplitude.
The embedding

\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\text{free, fin-len}}\subseteq\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\text{Tor-fin}}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Let us pick an object
F\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\text{Tor-fin}}
whose Tor-amplitude is in
[0,n]
for a non-negative integer
n
. We will prove that
F
lies in the image by
induction on
n
.

The base case
n=0
.
First, it follows from
[
10
,
Lemma 0654
]
that
F
is quasi-isomorphic to a one-term
complex consisting of a single flat module; second, by Lazard’s theorem, this flat module is filtered colimit of finitely generated free modules, and rewriting this colimit as a cokernel in the usual way gives a two-term resolution by free modules.

The induction step
.
Again, by
[
10
,
Lemma 0654
]
, we can assume that
F
is given by a length
n
complex
consisting of flat modules:

F\;\simeq\;\bigl(\;F_{0}\longrightarrow F_{1}\longrightarrow\ldots\longrightarrow F_{n}\;\bigr)\;.

The latter can be rewritten as a cofiber of a map between complexes of strictly smaller Tor-dimension:

\bigl(\;F_{0}\longrightarrow F_{1}\longrightarrow\ldots\longrightarrow F_{n}\;\bigr)\;\simeq\;\mathrm{cof}\Bigl(\;\bigl(F_{0}\longrightarrow\ldots\longrightarrow F_{n-1}\bigr)[-1]\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}F_{n}[-n]\;\Bigr)\;,

which lies in the image by the induction hypothesis combined with the fact that the subcategory is closed under finite colimits.
∎

Corollary 1.1.3
.

The embedding (
1.1.1.1
) induces a symmetric monoidal equivalence

\bigl(\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\text{free, fin-len}}\bigr)^{\mathrm{op}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\mathrm{Mod}^{\omega}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\;.

Proof.

Since the image of (
1.1.1.1
) contains the compact generators, the claim will follow as soon as we
show that the category
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\text{free, fin-len}}
is idempotent complete, which follows immediately from
Proposition
1.1.2
because the category
\mathrm{Mod}^{\text{Tor-fin}}_{A}
is idempotent complete.
∎

1.1.4.
Recollection on analytic rings

Here we, following Lecture 12 of
[
5
]
, briefly recall the notion of an analytic ring. Since working relative to the category

\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
of solid abelian groups, which was defined and studied in Lectures 5 and 6 of
[
3
]

(but we recall some of its properties in the beginning of
1.1
), is sufficient for the
purposes of this paper, we will give the definition relative to
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
.

Definition 1.1.4.1
.

(
[
5
]
, Proposition 12.20)
Let
R\in\mathrm{Ring}^{\leq 0}(\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}})
be a connective ring object in the category of solid abelian groups. An analytic ring
structure on
R
is a full subcategory

\textstyle{\mathcal{D}^{\leq 0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{*}}
\textstyle{R-\mathrm{Mod}^{\leq 0}_{\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}}

such that it’s closed under limits and colimits, stable under the functor
\uhom_{\mathrm{Mod}^{\leq 0}_{\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}}(\prod_{I}\mathbb{Z},-)
for any
compact generator
\prod_{I}\mathbb{Z}
, and the inclusion admits a left adjoint
j^{*}
satisfying

R\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}j_{*}j^{*}R\;.

∎

One of the main examples of an analytic ring is the following.
For a commutative finite type algebra (in usual abelian groups)
A
, the map
\mathbb{Z}\longrightarrow A
induces the restriction functor

(1.1.4.2)

\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\longrightarrow A-\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\;.

Theorem 1.1.5

(8.1 in
[
3
]
)
.

The functor (
1.1.4.2
) defines an analytic ring structure on the ring
A
.

1.1.6.
Solid tensor product and limits

Another technical observation, which we would like to leave here for future reference, concerns the solid tensor product and certain types of limits preserved by
it. It is one of the basic properties, listed at the beginning of
1.1
,
that tensoring with a compact object preserves products of copies of
A
, but we will need a stronger version of
that property and we package it into the following lemma.

Lemma 1.1.7
.

For a compact object
C\in\mathrm{Mod}^{\omega}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
, the functor

C\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}-:\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}

preserves right t-bounded products. That is, if
\{M_{j}\}_{j\in J}
is a collection of
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
-modules uniformly cohomologically bounded from above, then

C\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\prod_{J}M_{j}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\prod_{J}\bigl(C\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}M_{j}\bigr)\;,

Before we get to the proof of Lemma
1.1.7
, recall from
[
3
]
, Theorem 2.2, that the abelian heart of the category
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}

of solid abelian groups satisfies Grothendieck’s axiom (AB6). For the sake of clarity, we would like to state and prove a variant of that axiom that deals with
objects inside the derived categories. It will follow from the abelian variant combined with some basic properties of the categories and the t-structures.

Lemma 1.1.8
.

For any index set
J
and filtered categories
I_{j}
,
j\in J
, with functors
i_{j}\mapsto M_{i_{j}}
from
I_{j}
to
\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
,
the map

\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\prod_{j\in J}M_{i_{j}}\longrightarrow\prod_{j\in J}\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}M_{i_{j}}

is an isomorphism

Proof.

Since the standard t-structure on
\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
is non-degenerate, it suffices to check that the map induces an isomorphism on cohomology.
The latter reduces to the abelian (AB6) by the exactness of products and filtered colimits, which also follows from Theorem 2.2 of
[
3
]
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
1.1.7
.

Writing each
M_{j}
as a filtered colimit of compact objects inside
\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}^{\leq n}

and pulling these colimits out of the product by Lemma
1.1.8
,
we reduce the statement to the case where each
M_{j}
is compact. In this case, we
present each
M_{j}
as a finite length complex whose terms are non-zero only in degrees not greater than
n
and are given by products of
copies of
A
—see Corollary
1.1.3
—thereby
presenting
\prod_{J}M_{j}
as a (possibly infinite to the left) complex whose terms are products of copies of
A
. Finally, we finish the proof by writing the
latter as a filtered colimit of its naive truncations and using the fact that the solid tensor product distributes over products of copies of
A
.
∎

1.2.
Completions

In this subsection,
we recall how one usually defines completion
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I}
of
A
at an ideal
I\subset A
, and outline some favorable properties of
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I}
as a
commutative algebra in
\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
. It might be useful to keep in mind the following intuitive picture:

A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I}\simeq A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I}
,
and therefore
\spec^{an}(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I})\subseteq\spec^{an}(A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})
looks like a ‘Zariski’ open subset,
which should be thought of as a kind of a solid tubular neighborhood of
\spec(A/I)
inside
\spec(A)
. On the other hand,

A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I}
is compact as a solid
A
-module, which implies some additional functoriality between sheaves on
\spec(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I})
and
\spec(A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})
—specifically,
the embedding
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\subseteq\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
admits, besides a left adjoint, a continuous right adjoint. Thus from the point of view of the six-functor formalism,
\spec^{an}(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I})\subseteq\spec^{an}(A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})
behaves like a closed subset, and we shall tend to favor this point of view.

Definition 1.2.0.1
.

Let
Z\subset\spec(A)
be a closed subset, let
I_{Z}\subset A
denote its ideal. Let

\Gamma_{Z}\mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}\bigl(\Gamma(\spec(A)\setminus Z,\mathcal{O})\>/\>A\bigr)[-1]\;,

that is local cohomology of
A
with supports on
Z
. Recall from
[
10
,
Lemma 0A6R
]

that it can be represented by an extended alternating Čech complex:
let
f_{1}\ldots f_{l}\in A
be a set of generators of the ideal
I_{Z}
, then

\begin{split}\Gamma_{Z}=\Bigl(\Gamma(\spec(A)\setminus Z,\mathcal{O})\>/\>A\Bigr)[-1]&\;\simeq\;\Bigl(A\bigl[\frac{1}{f_{1}}\bigr]/A\Bigr)[-1]\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ldots\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\Bigl(A\bigl[\frac{1}{f_{l}}\bigr]/A\Bigr)[-1]\;\simeq\;\\
&\;\simeq\;\Bigl(\>A\to\bigoplus_{i}A\bigl[\frac{1}{f_{i}}\bigr]\to\bigoplus_{i<j}A\bigl[\frac{1}{f_{i}f_{j}}\bigr]\to\ldots\to A\bigl[\frac{1}{f_{1}\ldots f_{l}}\bigr]\>\Bigr)\;\;.\end{split}

Completion of
A
at
Z
, denoted
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}
or
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I_{Z}}
, can be expressed by the formula

A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}\>\mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}\>\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\bigl(\Gamma_{Z},A\bigr)\;\;.

More generally, completion of a module
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
is defined by the formula

M^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}\>\mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}\>\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\bigl(\Gamma_{Z},M\bigr)\;.

∎

Let us emphasize that, per our conventions on categories and functors, the above notion of completion is derived, not naive.

1.3.
Tubular neighborhoods and recollement

Let us fix a closed subset
Z\subset\spec(A)
. In the following couple of paragraphs we make a few observations about
nice solid-algebraic properties of the completion
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}
as an algebra object of
\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
and describe
a solid analog of the decomposition of the category of sheaves on a topological space with respect to a closed embedding and its complement.

First, let us note that
\Gamma_{Z}
has Tor-amplitude in
[0,\mathrm{dim}(A)]
—see

[
10
,
Lemma 0DXC
]
—and therefore

A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}\;=\;\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\Gamma_{Z},A)

is in the image of the functor

(1.3.0.1)

\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(-,A)\colon\bigl(\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\text{Tor-fin}}\bigr)^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\;;

it therefore follows from Proposition
1.1.2
that
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}
is a compact
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
-module.

In addition, since the functor (
1.3.0.1
) is symmetric monoidal—see
1.1
and in particular
1.1.1
—and

\Gamma_{Z}\otimes_{A}\Gamma_{Z}\simeq\Gamma_{Z}
,
the ring
A_{Z}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}
is tensor-idempotent over
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
, i.e.

(1.3.0.2)

A_{Z}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A_{Z}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}\simeq A_{Z}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}\;.

Those observations imply

1)

the forgetful functor

i_{*}\colon A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}

is fully faithful and its image is a tensor ideal inside
\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
;

2)

besides a left adjoint
i^{*}
, the functor
i_{*}
admits a continuous right adjoint

i^{!}\;=\;\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z},-)\>:\;\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\longrightarrow A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\;.

So there is a recollement

\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\>!}}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{\check{C}}_{Z\bullet}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\;,}
\scriptstyle{j_{*}}
\scriptstyle{j_{!}}

whose right hand we denoted by
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{\check{C}}_{Z\bullet}}
for the reasons soon to become apparent.
The following follows formally from what we already know:

1)

category
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{\check{C}}_{Z\bullet}}
is compactly generated by images of compact
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
-modules under
j^{*}
,
in particular, there is a set of
generators of the form
j^{*}\prod_{I}A\simeq\prod_{I}j^{*}A
;

2)

there is a unique tensor product on
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{\check{C}}_{Z\bullet}}
making the functor
j^{*}
symmetric monoidal.

To complete the picture, it remains to compute
j^{*}A
:

\textstyle{i_{*}i^{!}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{j_{*}j^{*}A}
\textstyle{\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A_{Z}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}},A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\simeq$}}}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$=$}}}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\simeq$}}}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$=$}}}
\textstyle{A/\Gamma_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\simeq$}}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathrm{\check{C}}_{Z}\;,}

where
\mathrm{\check{C}}_{Z}
denotes the complex of functions on
\spec(A)\setminus Z
.

In particular, in the case where the closed subset
Z\subseteq\spec(A)
is a hypersurface
\{f=0\}
, the category of solid

A
-modules decomposes as a recollement of
A_{f}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
and
\mathrm{Mod}_{A[\frac{1}{f}]_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
:

\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{f}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\>!}}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{A[\frac{1}{f}]_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\;.}
\scriptstyle{j_{*}}
\scriptstyle{j_{!}}

1.3.1.

Let us note for future reference that the isomorphism (
1.3.0.2
) is a manifestation of the fact that self-intersection of a tubular
neighborhood is given by the same tubular neighborhood. More generally, for a pair of closed subsets
Z_{1},Z_{2}\subset\spec(A)
,
there is an isomorphism

\Gamma_{Z_{1}}\otimes_{A}\Gamma_{Z_{2}}\;\simeq\;\Gamma_{Z_{1}\cap Z_{2}}\;,

which induces an isomorphism
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z_{1}}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z_{2}}\;\simeq\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z_{1}\cap Z_{2}}\>
.

1.4.
Localisation and local cohomology

Let us fix a multiplicative subset
S\subset A
, we will construct a box-like analytic ring structure on the ring
A[S^{-1}]

by constructing and describing the corresponding decomposition of the category of
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
-modules.

Recall from
1.1.1
that there is a pair of full subcategories

\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\text{Tor-fin}},\;\mathrm{Mod}^{\omega}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\;\subset\;\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\;,

and the functor

\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(-,A)\colon{\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{op}}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}

induces equivalences

\bigl(\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\text{Tor-fin}}\bigr)^{\mathrm{op}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\mathrm{Mod}^{\omega}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}

and

\mathrm{Mod}^{\omega}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\bigl(\mathrm{Mod}_{A}^{\text{Tor-fin}}\bigr)^{\mathrm{op}}\;,

which are inverse to each other.
In particular, this means that the functor
\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(-,A)
exchanges certain colimits and limits. For example, given a multiplicative subset
S\subset A
, there is a filtered colimit of cohomologies
\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\Gamma_{f}(A)=\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}A\bigl[\frac{1}{f}\bigr]/A\>[-1]
is of finite Tor-amplitude. Using the above duality, we obtain therefore isomorphisms

\begin{split}A[S^{-1}]/A\>[-1]&\;\simeq\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}A\bigl[\frac{1}{f}\bigr]/A\>[-1]\;\simeq\;\\
&\;\simeq\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{f},A)\;\simeq\;\\
&\;\simeq\;\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{f},A)\;.\end{split}

That leads us to the definition:

A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{S}\;\mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{f}\;.

As in
1.3
, it is a tensor-idempotent ring in
\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
, which is moreover compact as an
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
-module. Thus get a recollement

\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{S}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\>!}}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{A[S^{-1}]_{\bullet}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\;,}
\scriptstyle{j_{*}}
\scriptstyle{j_{!}}

whose right hand term
\mathrm{Mod}_{A[S^{-1}]_{\bullet}}
satisfies the following properties:

1)

\mathrm{Mod}_{A[S^{-1}]_{\bullet}}
admits a set of compact generators of the form

j^{*}\prod_{I}A\;\simeq\;\prod_{I}j^{*}A\;;

2)

for a single copy
j^{*}A
we have a fibre sequence

\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{S},A)\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow j_{*}j^{*}A\;,

from which it follows that
j_{*}j^{*}A\simeq A[S^{-1}]\>
;

3)

there is a unique tensor product on
\mathrm{Mod}_{A[S^{-1}]_{\bullet}}
making the functor
j^{*}
symmetric monoidal, and this tensor product distributes over
products of copies of
A[S^{-1}]
.

1.4.1.

We consider a variation that we will encounter while computing strata of the skeletal filtration. For a prime
\mathfrak{p}\subset A
,
we have a recollement

\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{A\setminus\mathfrak{p}}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\>!}}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}}-\mathrm{Mod}_{{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}_{\bullet}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\;.}
\scriptstyle{j_{*}}
\scriptstyle{j_{!}}

Indeed, it suffices to check that the cofiber of

\uhom_{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}}(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{A\setminus\mathfrak{p}},A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}})\longrightarrow A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}

is given by
{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Using the adjunction inside the local hom and pulling the completion out, we reduce it to
showing that

\mathrm{cofib}\bigl(\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{A\setminus\mathfrak{p}},A)\longrightarrow A\bigr)\;\simeq\;A_{\mathfrak{p}}\;,

which is a special case of
1.4
.

1.5.
Filtration by admissible subcategories

Definition 1.5.0.1
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a stable category. We say (cf.
[
11
]
) that a full subcategory
\mathcal{Z}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
is
admissible
if
the inclusion has both a left and a continuous right adjoint. Equvalently, we say that
\mathcal{Z}
is an
admissible quotient
of
\mathcal{X}
the localization functor
from
\mathcal{X}
to
\mathcal{Z}
admits a (fully faithful) right adjoint which in turn admits a continuous right adjoint. We shall typically denote the relevant functors

\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\>!}}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}}

∎

Example 1.5.0.2
.

For a closed subset
Z\subseteq\spec(A)
, the inclusion
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
defines an admissible subcategory—see

1.3
. Likewise, the induction functor

A_{Z}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}-\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\longrightarrow A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
defines an admissible quotient.
∎

Definition 1.5.0.3
.

We define a category equipped with a length
d

admissible filtration
as a functor

[0,d]^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow\stab

such that each arrow of the interval maps to an admissible quotient. Equivalently, a length
d
admissible filtration is a nested sequence of admissible subcategories

0=\mathcal{Z}_{-1}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{d}=\mathcal{X}.

We call
\mathcal{X}
the underlying category of the filtration.

We define
\mathrm{Fil}^{\textit{adm}}_{d}(\stab)
the category of length
d
admissibly filtered categories as the full subcategory of the category of all functors
[0,d]^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow\stab
spanned by the admissible filtrations.
∎

1.5.1.

Note that given two length
d
filtered categories
0=\mathcal{Z}_{-1}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{d}=\mathcal{X}
and
0=\mathcal{Z}^{{}^{\prime}}_{-1}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}^{{}^{\prime}}_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}^{{}^{\prime}}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{Z}^{{}^{\prime}}_{d}=\mathcal{X}^{{}^{\prime}}
, a functor
f:\mathcal{X}\longrightarrow\mathcal{X}^{{}^{\prime}}
either is incompatible with the filtrations or extends uniquely to a functor of filtrations. Put differently, for each
0\leq k\leq 0
, up to unique equivalence, there is at most one commutative square

\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{k}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}^{{}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{i^{{}^{\prime}}}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}^{{}^{\prime}}_{k}}

Indeed,
\mathcal{X}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\mathcal{Z}
is the cofibre of its kernel
U\mathrel{{\mathop{\hookrightarrow}\limits}}\mathcal{X}
, so to give a commutative square on the right is equivalent to giving a null-homotopy of the composite
U\mathrel{{\mathop{\hookrightarrow}\limits}}\mathcal{X}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\mathcal{X}^{{}^{\prime}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\mathcal{Z}^{{}^{\prime}}
. But such a null-homotopy if it exists is essentially unique, being an isomorphism between one continuous functor and the zero functor, which is final in the category of continuous functors.

1.5.2.
Cube associated with an admissible filtration

Consider a stable category
\mathcal{X}
equipped with an admissible filtration

(1.5.2.1)

0=\mathcal{Z}_{-1}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{d}=\mathcal{X}\;.

For an integer
k
denoting a level in the filtration, there is a recollement reconstructing
\mathcal{X}
from
\mathcal{Z}_{k}
and its complement, denoted
\mathcal{U}_{k+1}
:

\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{i_{k}}_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\>!}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{j_{k+1}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{U}_{k+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\;.}
\scriptstyle{{j_{k+1}}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{{j_{k+1}}_{!}}

For each
k
, we denote by
\mathcal{X}_{k}
the
k
th
stratum
—the quotient

\mathcal{Z}_{k}/\mathcal{Z}_{k-1}\;\simeq\;\mathcal{U}_{k}/\mathcal{U}_{k+1}
(cf.
[
8
]
, section 1.3); there are recollements:

\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{k-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{i_{k-1k}}_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\>!}_{k-1k}}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}_{k-1k}}
\scriptstyle{t_{k}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\;,}
\scriptstyle{{t_{k}}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{{t_{k}}_{!}}

\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{s_{k}}_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{U}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s^{\>!}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{s^{*}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{j_{kk+1}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{U}_{k+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\;.}
\scriptstyle{{j_{kk+1}}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{{j_{kk+1}}_{!}}

For each stratum
\mathcal{X}_{k}
, there is an adjunction

\textstyle{\Phi_{k}\colon\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{k}^{*}}
\textstyle{\;\mathcal{U}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s_{k}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{{j_{k}}_{*}}
\textstyle{\;\mathcal{X}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces:\rho^{k}\;.}
\scriptstyle{{s_{k}}_{*}}

We call the composite
\rho^{k}\Phi_{k}
the localization functor at level
k
and denote it by
L_{k}
.
The filtration produces a
(d+1)
-dimensional
fracture cube
,
which is a limit cube of functors whose vertices are given
by

[0\leq k_{0}<k_{1}<\ldots<k_{m}\leq d]\;\mapsto\;L_{k_{0}}L_{k_{1}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}

and whose edges, which correspond to inclusions of ordered sets

[k_{0}<k_{1}<\ldots<k_{m}]\;\subset\;[l_{0}<l_{1}<\ldots<l_{n}]\;,

are given by the natural transformations composed of the identities and the units
\id_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow L_{k}
.

Example 1.5.2.2
.

In the case where
d=2
, the cube looks like this:

\textstyle{L_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\id_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L_{0}L_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L_{0}L_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L_{0}L_{1}L_{2}}
\textstyle{L_{1}L_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
.

∎

1.5.3.

The fact that the cube is actually a (homotopy) limit cube can be proved either directly by induction on the length of filtration—this is classically
referred to as the cubical reduction principle, see section 2 in
[
7
]
or 8.B in
[
4
]
—or by
referencing the general language of stratifications introduced by D. Ayala et al.,
[
8
]
. In the latter case, in accordance with their
terminology, one should view an admissible filtration as a stratification by an interval, whose ‘closed’ strata are given by the
\mathcal{U}
s embedded into
\mathcal{X}
via
the shriek embedding.

1.5.4.

Reconstruction of an admissibly filtered category. Following
[
8
]
, we would like to recall how one can reconstruct a stable presentable category
\mathcal{X}
equipped with an
admissible filtration of the form (
1.5.2.1
) from its strata
\mathcal{X}_{k}
, the
gluing functors

(1.5.4.1)

\Gamma_{k<l}\mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}\Phi_{k}\rho_{l}\colon\mathcal{X}_{l}\longrightarrow\mathcal{X}_{k}\>,\quad 0\leq k<l\leq d\;,

and the natural transformations

(1.5.4.2)

\textstyle{\Gamma_{k<s}=\Phi_{k}\rho_{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{k<l<s}=\Phi_{k}\rho_{l}\Phi_{l}\rho_{s}}
\textstyle{\id\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\rho_{l}\Phi_{l}}

\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{l}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma_{k<l}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma_{l<s}}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma_{k<s}}
\scriptstyle{\Uparrow}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{k}}
.

The above data is used to construct the
gluing diagram
, which is a truncated
d
-dimensional cube of categories.
Its vertices are given by

(1.5.4.3)

[0\leq k_{0}<k_{1}<\ldots<k_{m}\leq d]\;\mapsto\;\mathrm{Fun}(C_{k_{0}<\ldots<k_{m}},\mathcal{X}_{k_{0}})\;,

where

C_{k_{0}<\ldots<k_{m}}\;\mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}\;\{[0\leq\alpha_{0}<\ldots<\alpha_{n}\leq d]:[k_{0}<\ldots<k_{m}]\subseteq[\alpha_{0}<\ldots<\alpha_{n}],\;k_{0}=\alpha_{0}\}\;\simeq\;[0,1]^{\times(d-k_{0}-m)}\;.

Its edges are given by

\begin{split}[k_{0}<\ldots<k_{m}]&\subset[l_{0}<\ldots<l_{n}]\;\mapsto\\
&\mapsto\;\;\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern where the formula
[\beta_{0}<\ldots<\beta_{r}]\mapsto\Gamma_{(\beta_{0}<\ldots<\beta_{r})\cap(\leq k_{0})}F((k_{0}\leq)\cap[\beta_{0}<\ldots<\beta_{r}])
does indeed
define a functor from
C_{l_{0}<\ldots<l_{n}}
because the functors
\Gamma_{-}
are functorial with respect to inclusions of subsets of
[0,d]

via (
1.5.4.2
).
Moreover, maps

\textstyle{C_{k_{0}<\ldots<k_{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{X}_{k_{0}})}
\textstyle{[\alpha_{0}<\ldots<\alpha_{r}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\in$}}}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{\alpha_{0}<\ldots<\alpha_{r}}\Phi_{a_{r}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\in$}}}

induce a functor

(1.5.4.4)

\mathcal{X}\;\longrightarrow\;\lim\Bigl([k_{0}<\ldots<k_{m}]\mapsto\mathrm{Fun}(C_{k_{0}<\ldots<k_{m}},\mathcal{X}_{k_{0}})\Bigr)\;.

The following statement follows directly from Section A.6 and the macrocosm case of Theorem A of
[
8
]
.

Proposition 1.5.5
.

The functor (
1.5.4.4
) is an equivalence.

Example 1.5.5.1
.

In the case where
d=2
, the cube looks like this:

\textstyle{\mathrm{Fun}(C_{0},\mathcal{X}_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0<1\to 0<1<2}
\scriptstyle{0<2\to 0<1<2}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{1}\to\Gamma_{1<2}\Phi_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fun}(C_{0<1},\mathcal{X}_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0<1<2}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fun}(C_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma_{0<1}\circ-}
\scriptstyle{1<2}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fun}(C_{0<2},\mathcal{X}_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0<1<2}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fun}(C_{2},\mathcal{X}_{2})\simeq\mathcal{X}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma_{0<2}\to\Gamma_{0<1<2}}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma_{1<2}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fun}(C_{0<1<2},\mathcal{X}_{0})\simeq\mathcal{X}_{0}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fun}(C_{1<2},\mathcal{X}_{1})\simeq\mathcal{X}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma_{0<1}}
,

where arrow labels of the form
1<2
or
0<1\to 0<1<2
mean restriction to a face of the source cube—for example,
C_{0}=\{0,\;0<1,\;0<2,\;0<1<2\}\simeq[0,1]^{2}
,

C_{0<1}=\{0<1,\;0<1<2\}
, and the arrow
0<1\to 0<1<2
is given by restricting a functor to the corresponding edge of
C_{0}
. Also, under the identification

C_{1}=\{1,1<2\}\simeq\{0<1,0<1<2\}=C_{0<1}
preserving the depicted order,
the arrow
\Gamma_{0<1}\circ-
is given by composing with
\Gamma_{0<1}
in the target, and the arrow
\mathcal{X}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(C_{0},\mathcal{X}_{0})
is given by the
square

\textstyle{\Gamma_{0<1}\Phi_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Phi_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{0<1<2}\Phi_{2}}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{0<2}\Phi_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
.

∎

1.5.6.

Moreover, if we assume that the category
\mathcal{X}
is symmetric monoidal, and, at each level

\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{i_{k}}_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{k}^{\>!}}
\scriptstyle{i_{k}^{*}}

of the filtration, we have a tensor localization (
\mathcal{Z}_{k}
is equipped with a symmetric monoidal structure
and the functor
i_{k}^{*}
is symmetric monoidal) such that the functor
{i_{k}}_{*}
satisfies the projection formula
(provided that such a symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathcal{Z}_{k}
exists, it is unique up to a homotopy and determined by the tensor product
on
\mathcal{X}
and the adjunction). In that case, each stratum
\mathcal{X}_{k}
canonically inherits the symmetric monoidal structure from
\mathcal{Z}_{k}
, and
it’s straightforward to check that the functors
\Phi_{k}
are symmetric monoidal. Therefore, the gluing functors are lax symmetric monoidal,
and so are the transition functors of the diagram (
1.5.4.3
), where we equip its vertices with the
pointwise symmetric monoidal structure.
Then it follows from the monoidal reconstruction theorem, which is Theorem 4.5.1 in
[
8
]
,
that the functor (
1.5.4.4
) is a symmetric monoidal equivalence.

1.6.
Decomposition with respect to a flag

The discussion below should be compared to
[
12
]
, introduction and section 2.2.
We give an example of an admissible filtration on the the category
\mathrm{Mod}_{{A_{\mathfrak{m}}}_{\bullet}}
—see
1.4
for the definition of the

\bullet
analytic ring structure—associated with a full flag in
\spec(A_{\mathfrak{m}})
. In this example, our constructions recover some classically known objects,
such as the higher dimensional local field associated with a full flag—see Example
1.6
. This serves solely an illustrative purpose and won’t be used
anywhere else in the paper.

For simplicity, we work here with a ring
A
of finite type over an algebraically closed field
k
and work over the ground category of solid
k
-modules in the
synthetic sense. See
0.1.3
). We consider a full flag

pt\simeq\>Y_{0}\subset Y_{1}\subset\ldots\subset Y_{d}=\spec(A)

of irreducible subvarieties. For simplicity, we treat here the case where each
Y_{i}
is smooth at
Y_{0}
. Let

\mathfrak{m}\subset A
denote the ideal of
Y_{0}\in\spec(A)
. We pick a set of generators
x_{1},\ldots,x_{d}\in A_{\mathfrak{m}}
of the maximal ideal of
A_{\mathfrak{m}}
such that
locally

Y_{i}\;=\;\{x_{1}=x_{2}=\ldots=x_{d-i}=0\}\;.

There is a filtration

0=\mathcal{Z}_{-1}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{d}=\mathcal{X}

on
\mathcal{X}=\mathrm{Mod}_{{A_{\mathfrak{m}}}_{\bullet}}
defined by

\mathcal{Z}_{i}\;\mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}\;(A_{\mathfrak{m}})^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d-i})}-\mathrm{Mod}_{{A_{\mathfrak{m}}}_{\bullet}}\;.

The strata are given by the formula

\mathcal{X}_{i}\;\mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}\;\mathcal{Z}_{i}/\mathcal{Z}_{i-1}\;\simeq\;(A_{\mathfrak{m}}\bigl[\frac{1}{x_{d-i+1}}\bigr])^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d-i})}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathfrak{m}}[\frac{1}{x_{d-i+1}}]_{\bullet}}\;.

In accordance with
1.5.2
, there is a fracture cube associated with this filtration—its vertices are given by

[0\leq k_{0}<k_{1}<\ldots<k_{m}\leq d]\;\mapsto\;L_{k_{0}}L_{k_{1}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}\;,

where
L_{i}
is an endofunctor of
\mathcal{X}
given by localizing from
\mathcal{X}
to
\mathcal{X}_{i}
and embedding back via the right adjoint.
It could be proved similarly that the conclusion of Lemma
2.3.4
holds true for these
L
s, which
allows for an immediate induction-based proof of the formula

(1.6.0.1)

L_{k_{0}}L_{k_{1}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}(A_{\mathfrak{m}})\;\simeq\;\Bigl(\ldots\Bigl(A_{\mathfrak{m}}\bigl[\frac{1}{x_{d-k_{0}+1}}]\Bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d-k_{0}})}\ldots\bigl[\frac{1}{x_{d-k_{m}+1}}\bigr]\Bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d-k_{m}})}\;.

Example 1.6.0.2
.

The longest composite
L_{0}L_{1}\ldots L_{d}
recovers the higher dimensional local field associated with the flag:

L_{0}L_{1}\ldots L_{d}(A_{\mathfrak{m}})\;\simeq\;k((x_{d}))\ldots((x_{1}))\;.

∎

1.7.
Mayer-Vietoris for tubular neighborhoods

Lemma 1.7.1

(Mayer-Vietoris formula)
.

For a pair of closed subsets
X,Y\subset\spec(A)
, the square

(1.7.1.1)

\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{X\cup Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{X\cap Y}}

is fibered.

Proof.

The square (
1.7.1.1
) is
A
-linear dual to the square

\textstyle{\Gamma_{X\cup Y}}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{X\cap Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\;,}

which is a fiber square by descent for local cohomology.
∎

Corollary 1.7.2
.

Let
Y\subseteq\spec(A)
be a closed subset decomposed as a union

Y=Y_{1}\cup\ldots\cup Y_{m}\;.

We claim that the functors

\{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{i}}\otimes_{A_{Y}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}}-:A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\longrightarrow A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{i}}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\}^{m}_{i=1}
are jointly conservative.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on
m
. The base case
m=1
is trivial. To prove the inductive step, let us pick a module
M\in A_{Y}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
which lies
in the joint kernel. We have

(1.7.2.1)

M\mathrel{{\mathop{\;\simeq\;}\limits}}M\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\mathrel{{\mathop{\;\simeq\;}\limits}}\lim\;\begin{pmatrix}&&M\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y^{\prime}}\\
&&\downarrow\\
M\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{m}}&\longrightarrow&M\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{m}\cap Y^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}\;,

where
Y^{\prime}=Y_{1}\cup\ldots\cup Y_{m-1}
. First,
M\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{m}}\;\simeq\;0
by the assumption of
M
lying in the joint kernel.
Likewise,
M\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{m}\cap Y^{\prime}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\;\simeq\;}\limits}}M\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{m}}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y^{\prime}}\;\simeq\;0
. Finally,
M\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y^{\prime}}\;\simeq\;0
by the assumption and the induction hypothesis.
So we conclude from (
1.7.2.1
) that
M\;\simeq\;0
.
∎

Corollary 1.7.3

(see also Example
1.7
)
.

Under the assumptions of Corollary
1.7.2
, there is an
m
-dimensional cube whose vertices are given by

\emptyset\subset[1\leq 2\leq\ldots\leq m]\;\mapsto\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\

and

[1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<\ldots<i_{l}\leq m]\;\mapsto\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{i_{1}}\cap\ldots\cap Y_{i_{l}}}\;,

and whose edges are the natural maps between completions. We claim that this cube is a limit cube.

Proof.

Note that the map from
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}
to the limit becomes an isomorphism after tensoring with each
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{i}}
—see
(
1.7.3.3
) in Example
1.7
. The claim thus follows from Corollary

1.7.2
.
∎

Example 1.7.3.1
.

In the case where
m=3
, the cube of
1.7.3
looks like this:

(1.7.3.2)

\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{1}\cap Y_{3}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{3}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}\cap Y_{3}}}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{2}\cap Y_{3}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
.

Tensoring (
1.7.3.2
) with
