A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{1}}
and using
1.3.1
to compute
vertices of the resulting cube, we get

(1.7.3.3)

\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{1}\cap Y_{3}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{1}\cap Y_{3}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}\cap Y_{3}}}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}\cap Y_{3}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
.

∎

1.8.
Tensoring with a compact preserves completions

There is a variant of Lemma
1.1.7
, which we will only need during the proof of the main theorem—Theorem
2.3.1
.
We record it here for future reference.

Lemma 1.8.1
.

For an ideal
I\subset A
and a compact object
C\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}^{\omega}
, the functor
C\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}-
preserves completion at
I

of a right t-bounded object:

C\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}M^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\bigl(C\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}M\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I}\;,

where
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}^{\leq n}
for some
n
.

We note that the lemma can easily be deduced from Proposition A.17 of
[
13
]
, yet we would like to give an independent proof
for the sake of completeness.

Proof.

Let us pick a set of generators
f_{1},\ldots,f_{r}\in I
of the ideal
I
, and let
K_{n}
denote the Koszul complex on
f_{1}^{n},\ldots,f_{r}^{n}
. According to

[
10
,
Tag 0913
]
, we can rewrite the local cohomology of
A
with supports in

V(I)\subseteq\spec(A)
, which is predual to the completion
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I}
, as a sequential colimit of duals to the Koszul complexes:

\Gamma_{I}\;\simeq\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}K^{\vee}_{n}\;.

Any sequential colimit, such as
\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}K^{\vee}_{n}
, can be rewritten in terms of a cofiber and direct sums:

\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}K^{\vee}_{n}\;\simeq\;\mathrm{cof}\Bigl(\oplus_{n}K_{n}^{\vee}\longrightarrow\oplus_{n}K_{n}^{\vee}\Bigr)\;,

which implies that the functor of completion
\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\Gamma_{I},-)
can be rewritten in terms of a fiber and products:

\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}K^{\vee}_{n},-)\;\simeq\;\mathrm{fib}\Bigl(\prod_{n}(K_{n}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}-)\>\longrightarrow\>\prod_{n}(K_{n}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}-)\Bigr)\;.

Now the claim follows from Lemma
1.1.7
.
∎

2.
The skeletal filtration

In this section, we introduce the skeletal filtration of the analytic spectrum of a ring
A
of finite type over
\mathbb{Z}
. Heuristically, the skeletal filtration at level
k
is given by the union of the tubular neighbourhoods of closed subschemes of dimension at most
k
. After constructing the filtration, we analyse the strata and prove the main theorem in the affine case.

2.1.
Skeleton rings

Now we define the rings whose spectra are given by unions of the tubular neighborhoods over the subsets of some fixed dimension.
These will enjoy all the same nice
properties that an individual tubular neighborhood does and will be used to produce an admissible filtration of
\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
.

Definition 2.1.0.1
.

For an integer
k
,
0\leq k\leq\mathrm{dim}(A)
, we define the
k
-
skeleton
ring

\begin{split}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\;&\mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}\;\>\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}\;\simeq\\
\;&\simeq\;\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\Gamma_{Z},A)\;,\end{split}

where the limit is taken over all subsets
Z\subset\spec(A)
of dimension up to
k
, and maps in the diagram are the maps between completions induced
by inclusions of the ideals.
∎

2.1.1.
The skeleton ring is compact and tensor-idempotent

Let us note that the diagram

(Z\subset\spec(A))\;\mapsto\;\Gamma_{Z}\;,

is filtered by union of closed subsets, and each
\Gamma_{Z}
has Tor-amplitude in
[0,\mathrm{dim}(A)]
,
see
[
10
,
Lemma 0DXC
]
; therefore,
the colimit has bounded Tor-dimension too, which, by Proposition
1.1.2
,
implies that
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}
is compact as a solid
A
-module. In addition, using cofinality of the diagonal, we can
show that the colimit is tensor-idempotent over
A
:

\begin{split}\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\Gamma_{Z}\;&\simeq\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\Gamma_{Z}\otimes_{A}\Gamma_{Z}\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\\
\;&\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\Gamma_{Z}\otimes_{A}\Gamma_{Z^{\prime}}\;\simeq\\
\;&\simeq\;\Bigl(\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\Gamma_{Z}\Bigr)\otimes_{A}\Bigl(\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\Gamma_{Z^{\prime}}\Bigr)\;,\end{split}

which implies that
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}
is tensor-idempotent over
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
.

2.1.2.

This is a lemma which describes intersection of a tubular neighborhood of a closed subset of dimension up to
k
with the
k
-skeleton.

Lemma 2.1.3
.

Let
Y\subset\spec(A)
be a closed subset of dimension
\leq k
. Then the canonical map

A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\;\longrightarrow\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\;\simeq\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Using cofilteredness of the limit, we write

\begin{split}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}&\;\simeq\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z\subset\spec(A)\\
\mathrm{dim}(Z)\leq k\\
Y\subseteq Z\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}\;\simeq\\
&\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\simeq}\limits}}\;\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z\subset\spec(A)\\
\mathrm{dim}(Z)\leq k\\
Y\subseteq Z\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}\;\simeq\\
&\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\simeq}\limits}}\;\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z\subset\spec(A)\\
\mathrm{dim}(Z)\leq k\\
Y\subseteq Z\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\;.\end{split}

∎

2.1.4.

The following lemma describes what happens when one intersects a tubular neighborhood of an irreducible closed subset
Y
of dimension
k
with
the
(k-1)
-skeleton. This will be used later in computing strata of the skeletal filtration; it also admits an interpretation in terms of existence
of certain analytic ring structures, see
1.4.1
.

Let
Y\subset\spec(A)
be an irreducible closed subset of dimension
k
, let
\mathfrak{p}\subset A
denote its ideal. Recall the notation

A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{A\setminus\mathfrak{p}}\mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{f}
, which was introduced in
1.4
.
We define a canonical map

A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k-1}\longrightarrow A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{A\setminus\mathfrak{p}}

as follows. Objects
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}
,
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k-1}
, and
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{A\setminus\mathfrak{p}}
are
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
-linear dual to discrete
A
-modules of finite tor-amplitude—
\Gamma_{Y}
,

\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\Gamma_{Z}
, and

\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}A[\frac{1}{f}]/A[-1]
respectively.
Therefore, by
1.1.3
and
1.3.1
, we have isomorphisms

A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k-1}\;\simeq\;\>\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y\cap Z}

and

A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{A\setminus\mathfrak{p}}\;\simeq\;\>\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{f}.

Finally, for
f\notin\mathfrak{p}
, Krull’s principal ideal theorem implies that
Y\cap Z(f)
has pure dimension
k-1
, so there is a canonical map

A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k-1}\;\simeq\;\>\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y\cap Z}\;\longrightarrow\;\>\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{f}\;\simeq\;\>A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{A\setminus\mathfrak{p}}

given by projecting onto the components in the limit on the right hand side.

Lemma 2.1.5
.

The canonical map

A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k-1}\longrightarrow A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{A\setminus\mathfrak{p}}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

This is a basic cofinality argument, i.e. it suffices, for each
Z\subseteq\spec(A)
of dimension
\leq k-1
, to find a function
f\in A\setminus\mathfrak{p}
such that

Y\cap Z\subseteq Y\cap Z(f)
. Any
f\in I_{Z}\setminus\mathfrak{p}
does the job.
∎

Lemma 2.1.6

(More limit formulas for
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}
)
.

1)

If
\spec(A)
is of pure dimension,
then the map

A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\;\longrightarrow\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}

is an isomorphism.

2)

The map

A_{k}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}\longrightarrow\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}A/I

is an isomorphism.

3)

Let
S\subset A
be the set of non-zerodivisors, then the map

A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}\;\longrightarrow\;\lim_{f\in S}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{f}\;,

where
d
denotes the dimension of
A
, is an isomorphism in case
\spec(A)
is reduced and of pure dimension.

Proof.

1)

This is a cofinality argument—one checks that each closed subset of dimension not greater than
k
is contained in a closed subset of pure
dimension
k
. That also uses the fact that the ring
A
is catenary, see
[
10
,
Tag 00NH
]
.

2)

This is almost tautological.

3)

This is also a cofinality argument—one checks that each closed subset is contained in the vanishing locus of a non-zerodivisor. That uses prime
avoidance, which says in this case that there is an element in the ideal of the closed subset, which doesn’t vanish at the associated points of
A
.

∎

2.2.
The skeletal filtration and its strata

Definition 2.2.0.1
.

For a non-negative integer
k\leq\mathrm{dim}(A)
, the
k
-
skeleton

\mathcal{Z}_{k}\mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}

is an admissible subcategory in
\mathcal{X}:=\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
, with embedding

{i_{k}}_{*}\colon\mathcal{Z}_{k}\;\hookrightarrow\;\mathcal{X}

admitting a left adjoint
i_{k}^{*}
and a continuous right adjoint
i_{k}^{!}
. Note that we use the fact that ring
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}
is tensor-idempotent over
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}

for the fully faithfulness and the fact that
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}
is a compact
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
-module for the continuity of the right adjoint—both facts were covered in

2.1.1
.
∎

Together the
\mathcal{Z}_{k}
s comprise the
skeletal
filtration

0=\mathcal{Z}_{-1}\subset\mathcal{Z}_{0}\subset\mathcal{Z}_{1}\subset\ldots\subset\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{dim}(A)-1}\subset\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{dim}(A)}=\mathcal{X}\;,

which is an admissible filtration
on the category of solid
A
-modules. (Recall from
1.5
the notion of admissible filtration.)
As in
1.5
, we denote by
\mathcal{X}_{k}
the stratum
\mathcal{Z}_{k}/\mathcal{Z}_{k-1}
, whose recollement with

\mathcal{Z}_{k-1}
reconstructs
\mathcal{Z}_{k}
:

(2.2.0.2)

\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{k-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{i_{k-1k}}_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\>!}_{k-1k}}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}_{k-1k}}
\scriptstyle{t_{k}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\;.}
\scriptstyle{{t_{k}}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{{t_{k}}_{!}}

We will also denote by
\mathcal{U}_{k}
the ‘complement’ to
\mathcal{Z}_{k-1}
inside
\mathcal{X}
:

\mathcal{U}_{k}:=\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Z}_{k-1}=\mathcal{X}/\mathrm{Im}\>{i_{k-1}}_{*}\;.

Again there are recollements—now reconstructing
\mathcal{X}
from
\mathcal{Z}_{k-1}
and
\mathcal{U}_{k}
, and each
\mathcal{U}_{k}
from
\mathcal{X}_{k}
and
\mathcal{U}_{k-1}
:

(2.2.0.3)

\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{k-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{i_{k-1}}_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\>!}_{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}_{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{j_{k}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{U}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\;,}
\scriptstyle{{j_{k}}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{{j_{k}}_{!}}

and

(2.2.0.4)

\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{s_{k}}_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{U}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s^{\>!}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{s^{*}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{j_{kk+1}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{U}_{k+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\;;}
\scriptstyle{{j_{kk+1}}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{{j_{kk+1}}_{!}}

all of the functors are continuous.

The following is a technical lemma describing what happens to a single completion
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}
under the action of the localization to
\mathcal{X}_{k}
.

Lemma 2.2.1
.

Let
Z=Z_{1}\cup\ldots Z_{m}\cup Z^{\prime}\subseteq\spec(A)
be a closed subset of dimension
k
with

Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{m}
being its dimension
k
irreducible components and
Z^{\prime}
a closed subset of dimension not greater then
k-1
.
Let
\mathfrak{p}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{p}_{m}
denote the ideals of
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{m}
. We claim that

{t_{k}}_{*}t_{k}^{*}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}\;\simeq\;{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\times\ldots\times{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}_{m}}\;.

Proof.

Observation 1
: For a closed irreducible subset
Y\subseteq\spec(A)
defined by a prime
\mathfrak{q}\subset A
of dimension
k
,

{t_{k}}_{*}t_{k}^{*}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\;\simeq\;{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{q}}\;.

Indeed,

\begin{split}{t_{k}}_{*}t_{k}^{*}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}&\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\simeq}\limits}}\;\>\mathrm{cof}\Bigl(\;{i_{k-1\>k}}_{*}i^{\;!}_{k-1\>k}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\;\longrightarrow\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\;\Bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;\mathrm{cof}\Bigl(\;\uhom(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k-1},A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y})\;\longrightarrow\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\;\Bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;\mathrm{cof}\Bigl(\;\uhom(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k-1}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y},A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y})\;\longrightarrow\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\;\Bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\;\simeq\;}\limits}}\mathrm{cof}\Bigl(\;\uhom(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{A\setminus\mathfrak{q}}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{q}},A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{q}})\;\longrightarrow\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\;\Bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\;\simeq\;}\limits}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{q}}\;.\end{split}

Observation 2
: For a closed subset
Y^{\prime}\subseteq\spec(A)
of dimension not greater then
k-1
,

{t_{k}}_{*}t_{k}^{*}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y^{\prime}}\;\simeq\;0\;.

Indeed,

\begin{split}{t_{k}}_{*}t_{k}^{*}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}&\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\simeq}\limits}}\;\>\mathrm{cof}\Bigl(\;\uhom(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k-1}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y^{\prime}},A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y^{\prime}})\;\longrightarrow\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y^{\prime}}\;\Bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\;\simeq\;}\limits}}\mathrm{cof}\Bigl(\;\uhom(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y^{\prime}},A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y^{\prime}})\;\longrightarrow\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y^{\prime}}\;\Bigr)\;\simeq\;0\;.\end{split}

Finally, we rewrite
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}
using the limit formula of
1.7.3
and note that, by the second observation above,
the functor
{t_{k}}_{*}t_{k}^{*}
vanishes on all of the vertices containing either
Z^{\prime}
or an intersection
Z_{i}\cap Z_{j}
. We therefore get

{t_{k}}_{*}t_{k}^{*}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}\;\simeq\;{t_{k}}_{*}t_{k}^{*}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z_{1}}\times\ldots\times{t_{k}}_{*}t_{k}^{*}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z_{m}}\;,

which concludes the proof by the first observation above.

The following illustrates the use of Corollary
1.7.3
in the case
m=2
:

\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z_{1}\cap Z_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z_{1}\cap Z^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z_{1}\cap Z_{2}\cap Z^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z_{2}\cap Z^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\mathrel{{\mathop{\mapsto}\limits}}}

\textstyle{{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{t_{k}}_{*}t_{k}^{*}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\;\;0\;\;\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\;\;0\;\;}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
.

∎

The following proposition describes the strata
\mathcal{X}_{0},\mathcal{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{dim}(A)}
as certain analytic rings. See also
2.2.3
.

Proposition 2.2.2
.

There is an isomorphism of rings
{t_{k}}_{*}t_{k}^{*}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\;\simeq\;\prod_{\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

Proof.

\begin{split}{t_{k}}_{*}t_{k}^{*}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}&\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\simeq}\limits}}\;\>\mathrm{cof}\Bigl(\;{i_{k-1\>k}}_{*}i^{\;!}_{k-1\>k}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\;\longrightarrow\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\;\Bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;\mathrm{cof}\Bigl(\;\uhom(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k-1},A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k})\;\longrightarrow\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\;\Bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\;\simeq\;}\limits}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}\mathrm{cof}\Bigl(\;\uhom(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k-1},A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z})\;\longrightarrow\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}\;\Bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\;\simeq\;}\limits}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in S}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}}\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;\prod_{\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}}\;.\end{split}

∎

2.2.3.

Let us note that it follows from the proposition combined with formal properties of the construction that
\mathcal{X}_{k}
defines an analytic ring structure
on the ring
\prod_{\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}}
whose compact generators are given by products of copies of the ring and the tensor product
distributes over products of compacts.

Example 2.2.3.1
.

If the ring
A
is integral, then, as an analytic ring,
the top stratum
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{dim}(A)}
is isomorphic to
K(A)_{\bullet}
, where
K(A)
denotes the fraction field of
A
. Recall from
1.4

that
\mathrm{Mod}_{K(A)_{\bullet}}
is compactly generated by products of copies of
K(A)
and has all the same formal properties that
\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
does.
∎

2.3.
Main theorem: the Beilinson-Parshin fracture cube

Recall from
1.5.2
that there is a fracture cube associated with an admissible filtration. In particular, the skeletal filtration
produces a
(\mathrm{dim}(A)+1)
-dimensional fracture cube,
which is a limit cube of functors whose vertices are given
by composites of the localization functors:

[0\leq k_{0}<k_{1}<\ldots<k_{m}\leq\mathrm{dim}(A)]\;\mapsto\;L_{k_{0}}L_{k_{1}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}\;.

Recall that, for a fixed level
k
, the functor
L_{k}
is given by localizing from
\mathcal{X}
to
\mathcal{X}_{k}
and embedding back via the right adjoint.

Theorem 2.3.1
.

The vertices of the cube are given by Beilinson-Parshin adeles. Specifically, each vertex is a continuous endofunctor that
is additionally right t-exact and preserves right t-bounded products and completions, and its value on
A
is given by the formula

(2.3.1.1)

L_{k_{0}}L_{k_{1}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}(A)\;\simeq\;\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{m}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{m}=k_{m}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>(\ldots\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{1}\supset\mathfrak{p}_{2}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{1}=k_{1}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{0}\supset\mathfrak{p}_{1}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{0}=k_{0}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}_{0}})_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\ldots)_{\mathfrak{p}_{m}}\;.

2.3.2.
Proof of the main theorem

The proof of the theorem consists of several parts and occupies the rest of this section. First, let us note that the ring

j_{k}^{*}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\;\simeq\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}}\;\>\in\>\mathcal{U}_{k}

is
tensor-idempotent and compact; the full subcategory
\mathcal{X}_{k}\subseteq\mathcal{U}_{k}
, being an image of
\mathcal{Z}_{k}
under
j^{*}_{k}
, is identified with

j_{k}^{*}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}-\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}\subseteq\mathcal{U}_{k}

(both categories are full subcategories in
\mathcal{U}_{k}
compactly generated by products of copies of
j^{*}_{k}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}
),
and the functor
L_{k}
is identified with

M\;\mapsto\;{j_{k}}_{*}(\Bigl(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}}\Bigr)\otimes_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}j_{k}^{*}M)\;.

For the sake of technical convenience,
we now introduce an auxiliary variant of the
L
-functor, which is given by the same formula but only with a support condition on the primes over which
the product is taken. Introducing the support condition will turn out to be useful in the proof of the formula (
2.3.1.1
) as it will
allow us to concisely phrase an induction on the length of the nested product.

Definition 2.3.2.1
.

For a prime
\mathfrak{q}\subset A
, an endofunctor
L^{\mathfrak{q}}_{k}\colon\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
is defined by the following formula:

L^{\mathfrak{q}}_{k}(M)\;=\;{j_{k}}_{*}(\Bigl(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}}\Bigr)\otimes_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}j_{k}^{*}M)\;.

Note that, substituting
\mathfrak{q}=0
into the formula for
L_{k}^{\mathfrak{q}}
, one gets back
L_{k}
.
∎

Lemma 2.3.3
.

The functor
L_{k}^{\mathfrak{q}}
is right t-exact.

Proof.

Observation 2.3.3.1.

The functor
j_{k}^{*}
is a right adjoint and monoidal. Besides, because

(2.3.3.2)

{j_{k}}_{*}j^{*}_{k}|\mathcal{Z}_{k}\;\simeq\;{i_{k}}_{*}{t_{k}}_{*}t_{k}^{*}\;,

it follows from Lemma
2.2.1
that
j_{k}^{*}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\;\simeq\;{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
∎

Using the observation, we get the following for
L_{k}^{\mathfrak{q}}
:

\begin{split}&\;L_{k}^{\mathfrak{q}}(M)\;\simeq\;{j_{k}}_{*}(\Bigl(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}}\Bigr)\otimes_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}j_{k}^{*}M)\;\simeq\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\simeq\;}\limits}}{j_{k}}_{*}j_{k}^{*}\Bigl(\Biggl(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\Biggr)\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}M\Bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\simeq\;}\limits}}{i_{k}}_{*}{t_{k}}_{*}t_{k}^{*}\Bigl(\Biggl(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\Biggr)\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}M\Bigr)\;.\end{split}

The claim reduces to showing that the functor
{t_{k}}_{*}t_{k}^{*}
is right t-exact, and since
\mathcal{Z}_{k}^{\leq 0}\>=\>A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}^{\leq 0}
is compactly
generated (by products of copies of
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}
), it suffices to check that
{t_{k}}_{*}t_{k}^{*}
is right t-exact on the generators, which follows from
Proposition
2.2.2
.
∎

Lemma 2.3.4
.

The functor
L_{k}^{\mathfrak{q}}
preserves right t-bounded products and completions.

Proof.

Products
:
\qquad L_{k}^{\mathfrak{q}}(\prod_{I}M_{i})\;\simeq

\begin{split}&\simeq\;{j_{k}}_{*}(\Bigl(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}}\Bigr)\otimes_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}j_{k}^{*}\prod_{I}M_{i})\;\simeq\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\simeq\;}\limits}}{j_{k}}_{*}j_{k}^{*}\Bigl(\Biggl(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\Biggr)\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\prod_{I}M_{i}\Bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\simeq\;}\limits}}{j_{k}}_{*}j_{k}^{*}\prod_{I}\Bigl(\Biggl(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\Biggr)\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}M_{i}\Bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\simeq\;}\limits}}\prod_{I}{j_{k}}_{*}j_{k}^{*}\Bigl(\Biggl(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\Biggr)\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}M_{i}\Bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\simeq\;\prod_{I}L_{k}^{\mathfrak{q}}(M_{i})\;.\end{split}

Observation 2.3.4.1.

By
[
10
,
Lemma 0DXC
]
, for any prime
\mathfrak{p}\subset A
, the local cohomology
\Gamma_{\spec(A/\mathfrak{p})}

has tor-amplitude in
[0,\mathrm{dim}(A)]
. And so does

\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}\Gamma_{\spec(A/\mathfrak{p})}\;,

it thus follows from Proposition
1.1.2
that its
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
-linear dual

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}

is a compact
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
-module. In particular, tensoring with this product
preserves certain products by Lemma
1.1.7
.
∎

The proof for completions is the same only we now use Lemma
1.8.1
to distribute the completion over the tensor product.
∎

For a pair of primes
\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}\subset\mathfrak{q}
, there is a natural transformation
L_{k}^{\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}}\rightarrow L_{k}^{\mathfrak{q}}
which is induced by the projection

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}}\longrightarrow\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}}\;.

Lemma 2.3.5
.

For a pair of primes
\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}\subset\mathfrak{q}
and a module
M\in A_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
, the map

L_{k}^{\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}}(M)\longrightarrow L_{k}^{\mathfrak{q}}(M)

is an isomorphism. The same is true for any composite of
L
s: for any ordered tuple
[k_{0}<k_{1}<\ldots<k_{m}]
, the map

L_{k_{0}}^{\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}}L_{k_{1}}^{\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}^{\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}}(M)\longrightarrow L_{k_{0}}^{\mathfrak{q}}L_{k_{1}}^{\mathfrak{q}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}^{\mathfrak{q}}(M)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

First we prove the claim for a single
L
. The category
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{q}}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
is compactly generated by products of copies of
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\>
, thus,
by Lemma
2.3.4
and continuity, it suffices to prove the claim
for
M=A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{q}}
.

\begin{split}L_{k}^{\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}}(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{q}})&\;\simeq\;{j_{k}}_{*}j_{k}^{*}\Bigl(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\Bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\;\simeq\;}\limits}}{j_{k}}_{*}j_{k}^{*}\Bigl(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\;\>\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}^{\prime},\;\mathfrak{p}\nsupseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}+\mathfrak{q}}\Bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;{j_{k}}_{*}j_{k}^{*}\Bigl(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\Bigr)\;\times\;{j_{k}}_{*}j_{k}^{*}\Bigl(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}^{\prime},\;\mathfrak{p}\nsupseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}+\mathfrak{q}}\Bigr)\;,\end{split}

where we used that

(2.3.5.1)

\begin{split}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}&\mathrel{{\mathop{\;\simeq\;}\limits}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\;\simeq\;}\limits}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{q}+\mathfrak{p}}\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\;\>\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}^{\prime},\;\mathfrak{p}\nsupseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}+\mathfrak{q}}\;.\end{split}

Each ideal
\mathfrak{p}+\mathfrak{q}
appearing in the right product has dimension not greater than
k-1
, thus the corresponding completion
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}+\mathfrak{q}}
sits on the

(k-1)
-th skeleton, i.e.
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}+\mathfrak{q}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{k-1}
, which is annihilated by
j^{*}_{k}
. Therefore, we have

\begin{split}L_{k}^{\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}}(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{q}})&\;\simeq\;{j_{k}}_{*}j_{k}^{*}\Bigl(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\Bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;{j_{k}}_{*}j_{k}^{*}\Bigl(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{q}}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}=k\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\Bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;L_{k}^{\mathfrak{q}}(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{q}})\;.\end{split}

The claim for a composite of
L
’s follows by induction on the length, where one may want to use
Lemma
2.3.3
and Lemma
2.3.4
to show
that a composite of
L
’s preserves the subcategory
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{q}}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\subseteq\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
.
∎

Now we are ready to finish the proof of Theorem
2.3.1
.

Proof of Theorem
2.3.1
.

The continuity holds by construction since each
L
is continuous, Lemma
2.3.3
shows the t-exactness, and
Lemma
2.3.4
shows that right t-bounded products and completions are preserved. As for the
formula (
2.3.1.1
), it will be convenient to prove the following slightly more general version of it: for any prime
\mathfrak{q}\subset A
,

L^{\mathfrak{q}}_{k_{0}}L^{\mathfrak{q}}_{k_{1}}\ldots L^{\mathfrak{q}}_{k_{m}}(A)\;\simeq\;\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{m}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{m}=k_{m}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>(\ldots\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{1}\supseteq\mathfrak{p}_{2}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{1}=k_{1}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{0}\supseteq\mathfrak{p}_{1}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{0}=k_{0}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}_{0}})_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\ldots)_{\mathfrak{p}_{m}}\;.

We will use induction on
m
. The base
m=0
is clear from the calculation at the beginning of
2.3.2
and
Definition
2.3.2
; we will now prove the inductive step.

\begin{split}L^{\mathfrak{q}}_{k_{0}}L^{\mathfrak{q}}_{k_{1}}\ldots L^{\mathfrak{q}}_{k_{m}}(A)\;&\mathrel{{\mathop{\simeq}\limits}}\;L^{\mathfrak{q}}_{k_{0}}L^{\mathfrak{q}}_{k_{1}}\ldots L^{\mathfrak{q}}_{k_{m-1}}\Bigl(\prod_{\mathfrak{p}_{m}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}_{m}}\Bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\simeq}\limits}}\;\prod_{\mathfrak{p}_{m}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}}L^{\mathfrak{q}}_{k_{0}}L^{\mathfrak{q}}_{k_{1}}\ldots L^{\mathfrak{q}}_{k_{m-1}}({\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}_{m}})\;\simeq\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\simeq}\limits}}\;\prod_{\mathfrak{p}_{m}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}}L^{\mathfrak{p}_{m}}_{k_{0}}L^{\mathfrak{p}_{m}}_{k_{1}}\ldots L^{\mathfrak{p}_{m}}_{k_{m-1}}({\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}_{m}})\;\simeq\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\simeq}\limits}}\;\prod_{\mathfrak{p}_{m}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>\Bigl(L^{\mathfrak{p}_{m}}_{k_{0}}L^{\mathfrak{p}_{m}}_{k_{1}}\ldots L^{\mathfrak{p}_{m}}_{k_{m-1}}(A)\Bigr)_{\mathfrak{p}_{m}}\;\simeq\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\simeq}\limits}}\;\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{m}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{m}=k_{m}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{m-1}\supseteq\mathfrak{p}_{m}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{m-1}=k_{m-1}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>(\ldots\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{1}\supseteq\mathfrak{p}_{2}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{1}=k_{1}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{0}\supseteq\mathfrak{p}_{1}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{0}=k_{0}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}_{0}})_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\ldots)_{\mathfrak{p}_{m-1}})_{\mathfrak{p}_{m}}\;,\end{split}

where we used Lemma
2.3.4
to pull products and completions through composites of
L
s, Lemma
2.3.5

to pass from
L
s supported on
\mathfrak{q}
to
L
s supported on
\mathfrak{p}_{m}
, and the induction hypothesis in the final row.
∎

3.
The main theorem: globalization

Let us fix a scheme
X
of finite type over
\mathbb{Z}
. Clausen-Scholze define the category
\text{QCoh}(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})

of solid quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
and prove that it satisfies descent (see Theorem 9.8 in
[
3
]
), which we now use to construct the skeletal
filtration on
\mathcal{X}=\text{QCoh}(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})
and prove a version of the main theorem for it.

3.1.
The skeleton’s behavior with respect to a Zariski localization

What now follows is a technical discussion describing behavior of the skeleton
with respect to a Zariski localization. Let
A\longrightarrow A^{\prime}
be a Zariski localization between finite type algebras.
The localization functor

\phi^{*}=A^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}-

admits a left adjoint
\phi_{!}
and a continuous right adjoint
\phi_{*}
, both of which are fully faithful. Let
V\subseteq\spec(A)

denote the closed subset

\spec(A)\setminus\spec(A^{\prime})
. The category
\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
is a recollement of
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{V}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
and
\mathrm{Mod}_{A^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
:

(3.1.0.1)

\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{V}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\kappa^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{A^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\;.}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{!}}

3.1.1.

Let us note that there is a fiber sequence

\phi_{!}\phi^{*}\longrightarrow\id\longrightarrow\kappa_{*}\kappa^{*}

resulting from the recollement (
3.1.0.1
), so it follows that
\phi_{!}\phi^{*}\;\simeq\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{V}/A[-1]\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}-
.

3.1.2.
Projection formula for
\phi_{!}

Because
\phi^{*}
is a monoidal colocalization, for each pair
K\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
,
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
,
there is a canonically defined map

(3.1.2.1)

\phi_{!}(K\otimes_{A^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\phi^{*}M)\longrightarrow\phi_{!}K\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}M

resulting from the adjunction. We claim that this map is an isomorphism. Indeed, by continuity, it suffices to check the claim on compact
generators. Substituting
K=\prod_{I}A^{\prime}\;\simeq\;\phi^{*}\prod_{I}A
and
M=\prod_{J}A
into (
3.1.2.1
), we get

\begin{split}\phi_{!}(K\otimes_{A^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\phi^{*}M)&\;\simeq\;\phi_{!}(\phi^{*}\prod_{I}A\otimes_{A^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\phi^{*}\prod_{J}A)\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;\phi_{!}\phi^{*}(\prod_{I}A\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\prod_{J}A)\;\simeq\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\;\simeq\;}\limits}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{V}/A[-1]\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\prod_{I}A\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\prod_{J}A)\;\simeq\\
