&\;\simeq\;(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{V}/A[-1]\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\prod_{I}A)\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\prod_{J}A\;\simeq\;\phi_{!}K\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}M\;.\end{split}

3.1.3.
\phi^{*}
respects the local hom

By adjunction, for a pair
M,\>N\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
, there is a canonically defined map

(3.1.3.1)

\phi^{*}\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(M,N)\longrightarrow\uhom_{A^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\phi^{*}M,\phi^{*}N)\;,

and it is an isomorphism. Indeed,

\textstyle{\text{Hom}_{A^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(L,\phi^{*}\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(M,N))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\text{Hom}_{A^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(L,\uhom_{A^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\phi^{*}M,\phi^{*}N))}
\textstyle{\text{Hom}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\phi_{!}L\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}M,N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\simeq$}}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq,\;(\ref{eq-projection-formula})}
\textstyle{\text{Hom}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\phi_{!}(L\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}^{\prime}}\phi^{*}M),N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\;.}
\scriptstyle{\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\simeq$}}}

3.1.4.

Fix an integer
k
and consider the skeleton rings
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}
and
{A^{\prime}}_{k}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}
. Denote by

i_{*}
and
i^{\prime}_{*}
the embeddings of the corresponding skeleton categories. The rest of
3.1
is devoted to showing that the functor

\phi^{*}\;=\;A^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}-

together with both of its adjoints nicely restricts to the skeletons. That is there is a diagram

\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{V}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{i^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\kappa^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\kappa^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{A^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{!}}
\scriptstyle{{i^{\prime}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{{i^{\prime}}^{!}}
\textstyle{A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{*}_{k}}
\textstyle{{A^{\prime}}_{k}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\phi_{k}}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{{\phi_{k}}_{!}}
\scriptstyle{i^{\prime}_{*}}
,

some of whose squares are naturally commutative and satisfy the Beck-Chevalley condition.
We will now explain how the dotted arrows are constructed and related to the other arrows in the diagram.

Lemma 3.1.5
.

\phi^{*}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\simeq{A^{\prime}}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}
.

Proof.

Because
\phi^{*}
preserves limits, it suffices to check that, for a closed subset
Y\subseteq\spec(A)
,

\phi^{*}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\;\simeq\;{A^{\prime}}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y\cap\spec(A^{\prime})}

(which is zero if the intersection is empty). Indeed, we have

\begin{split}\phi^{*}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\;&\simeq\;\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\Gamma_{Y},A)\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}\;\simeq\;\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\simeq\;}\limits}}\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}^{\prime}}(\Gamma_{Y}\otimes_{A}A^{\prime},A^{\prime})\;\simeq\;\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\simeq\;}\limits}}\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}^{\prime}}(\Gamma_{Y\cap\spec(A^{\prime})},A^{\prime})\;\simeq\;\\
&\simeq\;{A^{\prime}}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y\cap\spec(A^{\prime})}\;,\end{split}

where we used that

(3.1.5.1)

\Gamma_{Y}\otimes_{A}A^{\prime}\;\simeq\;\Gamma_{Y\cap\spec(A^{\prime})}\;,

see
[
10
,
Lemma 0G6I
]
.
∎

Corollary 3.1.6
.

There is an adjunction
\phi_{k}^{*}\dashv{\phi_{k}}_{*}
resulting from the map of rings
A_{k}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}\longrightarrow\phi_{*}\phi^{*}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\simeq{A^{\prime}}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}
.
The right adjoint is continuous and fully faithful.

Corollary 3.1.7
.

The Beck-Chevalley maps
\phi^{*}i_{*}\longrightarrow i^{\prime}_{*}\phi^{*}_{k}
and
\phi^{*}_{k}i^{!}\longrightarrow i{{}^{\prime}}^{!}\phi^{*}
are isomorphisms.

Proof.

For the map
\phi^{*}i_{*}\longrightarrow i^{\prime}_{*}\phi^{*}_{k}
,
the category
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
is compactly generated by products of copies of
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}
, and the involved functors are continuous
and preserve products; therefore, it suffices to check the formula on
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}
, which is the content of Lemma
3.1.5
.

The same argument applies to the other Beck-Chevalley map, though now we need to check that the map

\textstyle{\phi_{k}^{*}\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k},A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\uhom_{A^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}({A^{\prime}}_{k}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}},A^{\prime})}

is an isomorphism, which follows from
3.1.3
and Lemma
3.1.5
.
∎

Lemma 3.1.8
.

The functor
\phi^{*}_{k}
admits a (fully faithful) left adjoint
{\phi_{k}}_{!}
.

Proof.

First, we note that if
{\phi_{k}}_{!}
exists, then the composite
i_{*}{\phi_{k}}_{!}
is a left adjoint to

\phi_{k}^{*}i^{!}
. On the other hand, via the isomorphism
\phi_{k}^{*}i^{!}\simeq{i^{\prime}}^{!}\phi^{*}
, the left adjoint is given by

\vdash\phi_{!}{i^{\prime}}_{*}
, so
to prove that
{\phi_{k}}_{!}
exists it suffices to prove that the composite
\phi_{!}{i^{\prime}}_{*}
factors over
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
.
Note that
\phi_{!}{i^{\prime}}_{*}
is
continuous and, as it follows directly from
3.1.1
combined with Lemma
3.1.5
,
distributes over products of copies of
{A^{\prime}}_{k}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}
; it therefore suffices to prove that

\phi_{!}{A^{\prime}}^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\simeq\phi_{!}\phi^{*}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}
lives in the
image of
i_{*}
, which follows from the existence of the fiber sequence

\phi_{!}\phi^{*}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\longrightarrow A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\longrightarrow A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{V}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\;.

∎

Lemma 3.1.9
.

The Beck-Chevalley map
{\phi_{k}}_{!}{i^{\prime}}^{*}\longrightarrow i^{*}\phi_{!}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Follows from the lower star Beck-Chevalley condition of Corollary
3.1.7
by adjunction.
∎

3.2.
The skeletal filtration in the global case

Recall that
X
is a scheme of finite type over
\mathbb{Z}
. Using descent (see Theorem 9.8 in
[
3
]
),
we can write the category
\mathcal{X}\>=\>\text{QCoh}(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})
as a finite limit:

\text{QCoh}(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}\text{QCoh}(U^{\sigma}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})

where each
U^{\sigma}\subseteq X
is open in
X
and affine. An arrow
\sigma\longrightarrow\tau
in the diagram corresponds to an open inclusion

U^{\tau}\subseteq U^{\sigma}
of affines, the value of the arrow is the localization functor

\phi^{*}_{\sigma\tau}\colon\text{QCoh}(U_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}^{\sigma})\longrightarrow\text{QCoh}(U_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}^{\tau})
.
For an open affine
U\subseteq X
, we denote the
k
th skeleton of
\text{QCoh}(U_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})
, which is defined in
2.2
,
by
\mathcal{Z}_{k}(\text{QCoh}(U_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}))
. The discussion in
3.1
insures that we can form the limit

(3.2.0.1)

\mathcal{Z}_{k}\mathrel{{\mathop{\;=\;}\limits}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}\mathcal{Z}_{k}(\text{QCoh}(U^{\sigma}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}))\;.

Theorem 3.2.1
.

The categories defined by (
3.2.0.1
) produce an admissible filtration

(3.2.1.1)

0\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{dim}(X)}=\>\mathcal{X}\;.

Moreover, for an integer
k
and an open affine
U\subseteq X
, the localization
\mathcal{X}\longrightarrow\text{QCoh}(U_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})
fits into a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{k}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{k}(\text{QCoh}(U_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}))}
\textstyle{\text{QCoh}(U_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}_{U,k}}

which induces Beck-Chevalley isomorphisms for both the right adjoint to
i^{*}
and the further right adjoint.

Proof.

The discussion in
3.1

insures that Lemma
A.1.1
applies here, and the theorem follows.
∎

Corollary 3.2.2
.

Discussion from
1.5
automatically applies to the filtration
(
3.2.1.1
)—in particular, objects of
\mathcal{X}
admit fracture cube
decomposition, see
1.5.2
, and the category
\mathcal{X}
itself together with the symmetric monoidal structure
can be decomposed as a limit of a
\mathrm{dim}(X)
-dimensional cube, see
1.5.4
and
1.5.6
.

3.3.
Vertices of the cube in the global case

There is a limit cube associated with the filtration of Theorem
3.2.1
; like usual,
its vertices are given by composites of the form
L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}
for
0\leq k_{0}<\ldots<k_{m}\leq\mathrm{dim}(X)
.
Now our goal is to express the vertices in terms of nested completed localizations—as it was done in the affine case.
First, we note that it follows formally that category
\mathcal{X}
is compactly generated by the images of functors
{\phi_{\sigma}}_{!}
, which are the
left adjoints to the localizations
\phi^{*}_{\sigma}\colon\mathcal{X}\longrightarrow\text{QCoh}(U^{\sigma}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})
. In other words,
\mathcal{X}
is compactly generated by products
of copies the structure sheaf and the structure sheaves of tubular neighborhoods of closed subsets in
X
. By Lemma

2.3.4
and Corollary
A.2.2
, the vertices preserve right t-bounded products and completions, and,
in addition, they are continuous; thus it suffices to compute the value of
L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}
on the structure sheaf, which
is a special case of the following theorem.

Theorem 3.3.1
.

The value of
L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}
on a perfect complex
\mathcal{P}
is given by the formula

L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}(\mathcal{P})\;\simeq\;\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y_{m}\subseteq X\\
\mathrm{dim}Y_{m}=k_{m}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>(\ldots\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y_{1}\subset Y_{2}\\
\mathrm{dim}Y_{1}=k_{1}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y_{0}\subset Y_{1}\\
\mathrm{dim}Y_{0}=k_{0}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>\mathcal{P}_{Y_{0}})_{Y_{1}}\ldots)_{Y_{m}}

where the products are being taken over irreducible
Y
s.

Proof.

Let us denote the nested product on the right hand side by
L^{\text{BP}}_{k_{0}\ldots k_{m}}\mathcal{P}
.
By Theorem
2.3.1
, and continuity and co-continuity of each
\phi^{*}_{\sigma}
, for each
\sigma\in\Sigma
, there is an isomorphism

\phi_{\sigma}^{*}L^{\text{BP}}_{k_{0}\ldots k_{m}}\mathcal{P}\;\simeq\;L_{\sigma,k_{0}}\ldots L_{\sigma,k_{m}}\phi^{*}_{\sigma}\mathcal{P}\;.

On the other hand, by descent and Corollary
A.2.2
,

\begin{split}L^{\text{BP}}_{k_{0}\ldots k_{m}}\mathcal{P}&\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}{\phi_{\sigma}}_{*}\phi^{*}_{\sigma}L^{\text{BP}}_{k_{0}\ldots k_{m}}\mathcal{P}\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}{\phi_{\sigma}}_{*}L_{\sigma,k_{0}}\ldots L_{\sigma,k_{m}}\phi^{*}_{\sigma}\mathcal{P}\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}\mathcal{P}\;.\end{split}

∎

4.
Solid adelic descent

Let
X
be a scheme of finite type over
\mathbb{Z}
.
As a geometric application of our main results, we will prove a certain descent statement,
i.e. we will describe
X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
as a colimit (in the Tannakian sense) of a certain canonically defined cubical
diagram of affinoids, whose terms will be given by the adelic rings with the analytic ring structure inherited from
X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
.
These can be viewed as solid analogs of the results of
[
9
]
.

4.1.
Some properties of the
k
-completion

As usual, we fix a ring
A
of finite type over
\mathbb{Z}
.
We now study the functor of
k
-completion

\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\in$}}}
\textstyle{M^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}}
\textstyle{\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y\subseteq\spec(A)\\
\mathrm{dim}(Y)\leq k\end{subarray}}M^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\;,}
\scriptstyle{\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\in$}}}

where the limit is over the diagram of all closed subsets of dimension not greater than
k
.
We postpone some of the proofs until Appendix
B
.

Definition 4.1.0.1
.

For an integer
k
, an
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
-module
M
is called
k
-complete if the map

M\longrightarrow\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}M^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\;,

is an isomorphism. We note that, since the functor of
k
-completion is idempotent, an object of the form
M^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}
is
k
-complete.
∎

The following lemma provides a few non-tautological examples of
k
-complete modules.

Lemma 4.1.1
.

1)

For an ideal
I\subseteq A
of dimension
\leq k
and any
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
, the completion
M^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I}
is
k
-complete.

2)

For any
s<k
, an
s
-complete module is
k
-complete.

Proof.

Let
Z\subseteq\spec(A)
denote the closed subscheme defined by the ideal
I
. Recall that we denote by
\Gamma_{Z}
the local
cohomology of
Z
, which is the
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
-dual of
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}=A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I}
. We get

\begin{split}\bigl(M^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I}\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}&\;\simeq\;\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\Gamma_{Y},\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\Gamma_{Z},M))\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\Gamma_{Y}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\Gamma_{Z},M)\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;\uhom(\Gamma_{Z},M)\;,\end{split}

where the last isomorphism results from

A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}\;\simeq\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}\;,

see Lemma
2.1.3
.

The proof of the second statement is identical and uses

A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{s}\;\simeq\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{s}\;.

∎

Corollary 4.1.2
.

Rings
L_{k}(A)
and
L_{k_{0}}L_{k_{1}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}(A)
are respectively
k
and
k_{m}
-complete.

Proof.

Use 1) of
4.1.1
together with the formulas from
2.2.2
and
2.3.1
.
∎

These are the statements about completions, which will soon be used to obtain some nice properties of the adelic rings. The reader will find the proofs in

B.1.2
,
B.2.1
and
B.2.9
respectively.

Proposition 4.1.3
.

The set of functors

\{(-)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\in\spec(A)\\
\mathrm{dim}(\mathfrak{p})\leq k\end{subarray}}

is jointly conservative on the full subcategory of
k
-complete modules.

Proposition 4.1.4

(cf. Proposition A.17 in
[
13
]
)
.

1)

The functor of
k
-completion is symmetric monoidal;

2)

for an ideal
I\subset A
, the functor taking an
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
-module to its
I
-completion is symmetric monoidal.

In particular, solid tensor product of a pair of
k
-complete (
I
-complete)
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
-modules is
k
-complete (
I
-complete).

4.2.
Solid adelic descent

In this section we state and prove a descent statement, which can be viewed as a solid analog of Theorem 0.2 in
[
9
]
.
Recall that
X
denotes a scheme of finite type over
\mathbb{Z}
. First, we establish certain nice properties of the adelic rings in the solid setting.

Proposition 4.2.1
.

For an ordered tuple
[0\leq k_{0}<\ldots<k_{m}\leq\mathrm{dim}(X)]
, the canonical map

L_{k_{0}}\mathcal{O}_{X}\otimes_{X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ldots\otimes_{X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}L_{k_{m}}\mathcal{O}_{X}\longrightarrow L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}\mathcal{O}_{X}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the statement in the case where
X=\spec(A)
for a finite type algebra
A
.
Moreover, by induction, it suffices to prove that the map

(4.2.1.1)

L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m-1}}A\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}L_{k_{m}}A\;\longrightarrow\;L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}A

is an isomorphism.
Now since both source and target are
k_{m}
-complete by Lemma
4.1.1
and Proposition
4.1.4
,
by the joint conservativeness of Proposition
4.1.3
,
it suffices to prove that (
4.2.1.1
) becomes an isomorphism upon application of the functor of
\mathfrak{p}
-completion for each prime

\mathfrak{p}\in\spec(A)
of dimension not greater than
k_{m}
.

First, we compute the
\mathfrak{p}
-completion of the left hand side of (
4.2.1.1
). By
4.1.4
,

\bigl(L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m-1}}A\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}L_{k_{m}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\;\simeq\;\bigl(L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m-1}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\bigl(L_{k_{m}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\;.

Using
2.2.2
,

\bigl(L_{k_{m}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\;\simeq\;\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{m}\\
\mathrm{dim}(\mathfrak{p}_{m})=k_{m}\end{subarray}}\bigl({\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}_{m}}\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\;\simeq\;\left\{\begin{smallmatrix}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}}\>,\text{ if }\mathrm{dim}(\mathfrak{p})=k_{m};\\
0\text{ otherwise.}\end{smallmatrix}\right.

Given that the result vanishes unless
\mathrm{dim}(\mathfrak{p})=k_{m}
, while computing the other multiple
\bigl(L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m-1}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
, we can assume that

\mathrm{dim}(\mathfrak{p})=k_{m}
. Using Theorem
2.3.1
,

\begin{split}\bigl(L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m-1}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}&\;\simeq\;\Bigl(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{m-1}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{m-1}=k_{m-1}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>(\ldots\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{1}\supset\mathfrak{p}_{2}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{1}=k_{1}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{0}\supset\mathfrak{p}_{1}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{0}=k_{0}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}_{0}})_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\ldots)_{\mathfrak{p}_{m-1}}\Bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\\
&\;\simeq\;\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{m-1}\supset\mathfrak{p}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{m-1}=k_{m-1}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>(\ldots\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{1}\supset\mathfrak{p}_{2}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{1}=k_{1}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{0}\supset\mathfrak{p}_{1}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{0}=k_{0}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}_{0}})_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\ldots)_{\mathfrak{p}_{m-1}}\end{split}

Putting the last two computations together and using
4.1.4
one more time, we get

\bigl(L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m-1}}A\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}L_{k_{m}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\;\simeq\;{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>\Bigl(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{m-1}\supset\mathfrak{p}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{m-1}=k_{m-1}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>(\ldots\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{1}\supset\mathfrak{p}_{2}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{1}=k_{1}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{0}\supset\mathfrak{p}_{1}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{0}=k_{0}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}_{0}})_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\ldots)_{\mathfrak{p}_{m-1}}\Bigr)_{\mathfrak{p}}

Similarly, using Theorem
2.3.1
, we get that the
\mathfrak{p}
-completion
\bigl(L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
of right hand side of
(
4.2.1.1
) vanishes, unless
\mathrm{dim}(\mathfrak{p})=k_{m}
, and in the latter case is equal to

{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>\Bigl(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{m-1}\supset\mathfrak{p}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{m-1}=k_{m-1}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>(\ldots\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{1}\supset\mathfrak{p}_{2}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{1}=k_{1}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}_{0}\supset\mathfrak{p}_{1}\\
\mathrm{dim}\mathfrak{p}_{0}=k_{0}\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}_{0}})_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\ldots)_{\mathfrak{p}_{m-1}}\Bigr)_{\mathfrak{p}}\;.

∎

Corollary 4.2.2
.

The ring
L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}\mathcal{O}_{X}
is idempotent relative to
\text{QCoh}(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})
.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.2.1
, it suffices to prove that
L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}
is an idempotent algebra for each
k
.
Moreover, by descent, it suffices to treat the affine case
X=\spec(A)
.
By
4.1.1
and
4.1.4
, in order to prove that the map

(4.2.2.1)

L_{k}A\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}L_{k}A\;\longrightarrow\;L_{k}A

is an isomorphism, it suffices to prove that it becomes so after the
\mathfrak{p}
-completion for each prime
\mathfrak{p}
of dimension not greater than
k
.
Using
2.2.2
,
4.1.4
, and also the fact that completion preserves products, we get that
both source and target of (
4.2.2.1
) vanish under the
\mathfrak{p}
-completion unless
\mathrm{dim}(\mathfrak{p})=k
, and in the latter case (
4.2.2.1
)
turns into

{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}}\;\longrightarrow\;{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{p}}\;,

which is an isomorphism because the functor of
\mathfrak{p}
-completion is symmetric monoidal and
A_{\mathfrak{p}}
is idempotent over
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
.
∎

4.2.3.

We are now ready to state and prove the descent statment.
For an ordered tuple
[0\leq k_{0}<\ldots<k_{m}\leq\mathrm{dim}(X)]
, we consider a ring

L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}\mathcal{O}_{X}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\text{QCoh}(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}))\;.

As before, the unit maps
\id\longrightarrow L_{k}
produce us a map of rings

L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}\mathcal{O}_{X}\longrightarrow L_{l_{0}}\ldots L_{l_{n}}\mathcal{O}_{X}

for each inclusion

[k_{0}<k_{1}<\ldots<k_{m}]\;\subset\;[l_{0}<l_{1}<\ldots<l_{n}]\;.

Theorem 4.2.4

(Solid adelic descent)
.

The map

(4.2.4.1)

\text{QCoh}(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})\longrightarrow\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}\mathcal{O}_{X}-\mathrm{Mod}_{\text{QCoh}(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Let us denote the functor (
4.2.4.1
) by
F^{*}
and its right adjoint by
F_{*}
. Likewise, we write

F^{*}_{k_{0}\ldots k_{m}}
for the functor

\text{QCoh}(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})\longrightarrow L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}\mathcal{O}_{X}-\mathrm{Mod}_{\text{QCoh}(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})}

and
{F_{k_{0}\ldots k_{m}}}_{*}
for its right adjoint.

Let us prove first that
F^{*}
is fully faithful. The unit
id\longrightarrow F_{*}F^{*}
is given by

\begin{split}M&\;\mapsto\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}{F_{k_{0}\ldots k_{m}}}_{*}F^{*}_{k_{0}\ldots k_{m}}M\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}M\otimes_{X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}{F_{k_{0}\ldots k_{m}}}_{*}F^{*}_{k_{0}\ldots k_{m}}\mathcal{O}_{X}\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;M\otimes_{X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}\mathcal{O}_{X}\;.\end{split}

So it suffices to prove that

\mathcal{O}_{X}\longrightarrow\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}\mathcal{O}_{X}

is an isomorphism, which is a direct corollary of Theorems
3.2.1
and
3.3.1
.

To finish the proof of the proposition, we need to check that the counit
F^{*}F_{*}\longrightarrow\id
is an isomorphism, but it follows immideately from Corollary
4.2.2
.
∎

Remark 4.2.4.2
.

The above theorem together with
4.2.2
and
4.2.1
mean that the map

\spec_{X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(L_{0}\mathcal{O}_{X})\sqcup\ldots\sqcup\spec_{X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(L_{\mathrm{dim}(X)}\mathcal{O}_{X})\;\longrightarrow\;X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}

a closed !-covering, whose Čech nerve is given by that cubical diagram of Proposition
4.2.4
. We will prove in
4.3
that each piece of this cover is in fact affinoid.
∎

4.3.
The adelic opens are affinoid

Now we would like to prove that each term
L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}\mathcal{O}_{X}-\mathrm{Mod}_{\text{QCoh}(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})}
is in fact a category of modules over an analytic ring.
Let
\Gamma(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}},-)
denote the push-forward along
X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}\longrightarrow\spec^{\text{an}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})
. It induces a functor

(4.3.0.1)

L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}\mathcal{O}_{X}-\mathrm{Mod}_{\text{QCoh}(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})}\longrightarrow\Gamma(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}},L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}\mathcal{O}_{X})-\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\;.

Proposition 4.3.1
.

a)

The ring
\Gamma(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}},L_{k_{0}}\ldots L_{k_{m}}\mathcal{O}_{X})
is connective;

b)

the functor (
4.3.0.1
) is fully faithful and admits a symmetric monoidal left adjoint.

The connectivity simply follows from
2.3.1
, exactness of products in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
,
and cocontinuity of the functor
\Gamma(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}},-)
. The proof of the fully faithfulness part of

4.3.1
.b
occupies the rest of this section. To simplify our notation, we will only consider the case
m=0
; the argument will work in the general case without modification.

Lemma 4.3.2
.

Let
U\subseteq X
be a Zariski open. The diagram

\textstyle{L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}-\mathrm{Mod}_{\text{QCoh}(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}},-)}
\scriptstyle{L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U}\otimes_{L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}}-}
\textstyle{\Gamma(L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X})-\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma(L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U})\otimes_{\Gamma(L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X})}-}
\textstyle{L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U}-\mathrm{Mod}_{\text{QCoh}(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}},-)}
\textstyle{\Gamma(L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U})-\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\;,}

where

L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U}\;\mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}\;\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\in U\\
\mathrm{dim}(\mathfrak{p})=k\end{subarray}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathfrak{p}}\;\simeq\;\bigl(L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}\bigr)|_{U_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\;,

is commutative via the Beck-Chevalley map.

Proof.

First, we note that all the functors are continuous, so it suffices to prove that diagram commutes on modules of the form
L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}\otimes_{X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}M
.
Now since the ring
L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}
splits as a product

L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}\;\simeq\;\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\in X\\
\mathrm{dim}(\mathfrak{p})=k\end{subarray}}\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathfrak{p}}\;\simeq\;\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\in U\\
\mathrm{dim}(\mathfrak{p})=k\end{subarray}}\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathfrak{p}}\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\in X\setminus U\\
\mathrm{dim}(\mathfrak{p})=k\end{subarray}}\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathfrak{p}}\;=:\;L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U}\times R\;,

we have

\textstyle{L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}\otimes_{X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}M\simeq(L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U}\otimes M)\times(R\otimes M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma(L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U}\otimes M)\times\Gamma(R\otimes M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma(L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U})\otimes_{\left(\Gamma(L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U})\times\Gamma(R)\right)}-}
\textstyle{L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U}\otimes M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma(L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U}\otimes M)\;.}

∎

We now present
X
as a finite colimit (in classical schemes)

\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}U_{j}\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\;X\;,

where each
U_{j}\subseteq X
is an affine open.

Lemma 4.3.3
.

The functor

L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}-\mathrm{Mod}_{\text{QCoh}(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})}\mathrel{{\mathop{\>\;\longrightarrow\;\>}\limits}}{\lim_{j}}^{*}\;\>L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U_{j}}-\mathrm{Mod}_{\text{QCoh}(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})}\;,

where the limit is computed via the upper-
*
functors, is fully faithful.

Proof.

By the projection formula, it suffices to prove that the map

(4.3.3.1)

L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}\;\longrightarrow\;\lim_{j}L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U_{j}}

is an isomorphism, which is true because
\{U_{j}{}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}\longrightarrow X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}\}_{j}
satisfies upper-
*
descent and
L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U_{j}}\simeq\bigl(L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}\bigr)|_{{U_{j}}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
.
∎

Applying
\Gamma(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}},-)
to (
4.3.3.1
), by the same argument we get the following lemma.

Lemma 4.3.4
.

The functor

\Gamma(L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X})-\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\>\;\longrightarrow\;\>}\limits}}{\lim_{j}}^{*}\;\>\Gamma(L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U_{j}})-\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}

is fully faithful. Again, all the functors are the upper-
*
functors.

Now we are ready to finish the proof of Proposition
4.3.1
.

Proof of
4.3.1
b
.

Let us take a look at the right adjoint to the upper-
*
functor

{\lim_{j}}^{*}\;\>\Gamma(L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U_{j}})-\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\;\>\longrightarrow\;\>{\lim_{j}}^{*}\;\>L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U_{j}}-\mathrm{Mod}_{\text{QCoh}(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})}\;.

By Lemma
4.3.2
, it is given by the limit of the right adjoints and fits into a commutative square

\textstyle{L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}-\mathrm{Mod}_{\text{QCoh}(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}},-)}
\textstyle{\Gamma(L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X})-\mathrm{Mod}_{Z_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\lim_{j}}^{*}\;\>L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U_{j}}-\mathrm{Mod}_{\text{QCoh}(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\lim_{j}}^{*}\;\>\Gamma(L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U_{j}})-\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\;.}

On the other hand, by affinness of
U_{j}
, each right adjoint
\Gamma\colon L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U_{j}}-\mathrm{Mod}_{\text{QCoh}(X_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})}\longrightarrow\Gamma(L_{k}\mathcal{O}_{U_{j}})-\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
is fully faithful, and therefore, by
4.3.3
and
4.3.4
, the top horizontal
arrow of the square must be fully faithful.
∎

Appendix A
Limits of admissibly filtered categories

In this section, we study diagrams of admissibly filtered categories (see
1.5
) and
limits thereof, and we answer the following questions: under what conditions a diagram
of admissibly filtered categories induces an admissible filtration in the limit,
and if it does, what can be said about the cube vertices of the induced filtration.

A.1.

Fix a diagram

\textstyle{\Sigma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Fil}^{\textit{adm}}_{d}(\stab)}
\textstyle{\sigma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\in$}}}
\textstyle{\bigl(0=\mathcal{Z}_{\sigma,-1}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{\sigma,0}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{\sigma,d}=\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\in$}}}

of length
d
admissible filtrations. For each arrow
\sigma\to\tau\in\Sigma
,
there is a functor
\phi_{\sigma\tau}^{*}\colon\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}\longrightarrow\mathcal{X}_{\tau}
, inducing a commutative diagram

(A.1.0.1)

\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{\sigma,k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{*}_{\sigma\tau,k}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}_{\sigma,k}}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{*}_{\sigma\tau}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{\tau,k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{\tau}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}_{\tau,k}}

at a level
k
in the filtration. Note that, by the discussion in
1.5.1
,
each of these induced functors
\phi^{*}_{\sigma\tau,k}
is unique up to a homotopy.
We will assume that, for each
k
, the diagram (
A.1.0.1
) induces Beck-Chevalley isomorphisms

\phi^{*}_{\sigma\tau}{i_{\sigma,k}}_{*}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{i_{\tau,k}}_{*}\phi^{*}_{\sigma\tau,k}
and

\phi^{*}_{\sigma\tau,k}i^{!}_{\sigma,k}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}i^{!}_{\tau,k}\phi^{*}_{\sigma\tau}
.

Lemma A.1.1
.

The limit
\mathcal{X}=\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}
has an induced admissible filtration

(A.1.1.1)

\textstyle{\{\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}\mathcal{Z}_{\sigma,k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{i_{k}}_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\}}
\scriptstyle{i_{k}^{!}}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}_{k}}

Moreover, for each pair
(\sigma,k)
, the diagram

(A.1.1.2)

\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\sigma,k}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{k}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{*}_{\sigma}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{\sigma,k}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}_{\sigma,k}}

commutes and induces Beck-Chevalley isomorphisms for the adjoints
{i_{k}}_{*}
and
i^{!}_{k}
.

Proof.

By Lemma 2.6.4 of
[
14
]
, the adjoints
{i_{k}}_{*}
and
i_{k}^{!}
exist and satisfy the Beck-Chevalley conditions. It remains to check that
the functor
{i_{k}}_{*}
is fully faithful, which can be easily done using the limit formula for mapping spaces.
∎

A.2.
Vertices of the limit cube

Here we continue to work under the conditions of
A.1
. Recall that, for a level
k
in the filtration,
the stratum
\mathcal{X}_{k}
is defined by the recollement

(A.2.0.1)

\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{k-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{i_{k-1k}}_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{k-1k}^{!}}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}_{k-1k}}
\scriptstyle{t_{k}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\;,}
\scriptstyle{{t_{k}}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{{t_{k}}_{!}}

and the localization functor
L_{k}
is given by localizing to
\mathcal{X}_{k}
and embedding back to
\mathcal{X}
:

L_{k}\>=\>\rho_{k}\Phi_{k}\>=\>{i_{k}}_{*}{t_{k}}_{*}t_{k}^{*}i_{k}^{*}\;,

\textstyle{\Phi_{k}\colon\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{k}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{i_{k}}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{t_{k}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces:\rho_{k}\;.}
\scriptstyle{{t_{k}}_{*}}

Let us note that, by virtue of Lemma
A.1.1
, the functors
i_{k}^{*}
and
{i_{k}}_{*}
are given by the limits of

i_{\sigma,k}^{*}
and
{i_{\sigma,k}}_{*}
correspondingly. Similarly, the functors
i_{k-1k}^{!}
and
{i_{k-1k}}_{*}
are given by the limits of

i_{\sigma,k-1k}^{!}
and
{i_{\sigma,k-1k}}_{*}
. Furthermore, because taking a limit preserves recollements, which immediately follows from the fact that it preserves
fibers between stable categories, the functors
{t_{k}}_{*}
and
t_{k}^{*}
are given by the limits of the functors
{t_{\sigma,k}}_{*}
and
t_{\sigma,k}^{*}
correspondingly.
Putting all this together, we get that the functor
L_{k}
is given by the limit of the functors
L_{\sigma,k}={i_{\sigma,k}}_{*}{t_{\sigma,k}}_{*}t_{\sigma,k}^{*}i_{\sigma,k}^{*}
.

Lemma A.2.1
.

We have

L_{k}\;\simeq\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}L_{\sigma,k}\;,

where the limit is taken is the category of stable presentable categories. Moreover, the same is true for any composite of the Bousfield localization
functors, and thus it is true for the fracture cube—the fracture cube of the limit filtration is given by the limit of the fracture cubes.

Corollary A.2.2
.

For each pair
(\sigma,k)
, we have a canonically defined isomorphism of the form

\phi^{*}_{\sigma}L_{k}\;\simeq\;L_{\sigma,k}\phi_{\sigma}^{*}\;.

Moreover, in case each
\phi_{\sigma}^{*}
has a right adjoint
{\phi_{\sigma}}_{*}
and we have

L_{k}\;\simeq\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}{\phi_{\sigma}}_{*}L_{\sigma,k}\phi^{*}_{\sigma}\;,

where the limit is taken in the category of endofunctors of
\mathcal{X}
.

Appendix B
Certain properties of the
k
-completion and
k
-complete solid modules

In this appendix we establish certain properties of the
k
-completion and
k
-complete modules—the reader can find the definitions at the beginning of
section
4
. As usual, in this section,
A
denotes a commutative ring of finite type over
\mathbb{Z}
.

B.1.

Lemma B.1.1
.

The canonical map

(B.1.1.1)

M^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\longrightarrow\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}M^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Since the functor of
k
-completion is given by

\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k},A),-)

and the RHS of (
B.1.1.1
) is given by

\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}},A),-)\;,

it suffices to prove the statement in the case where
M=A
.

It follows from Proposition
2.2.2
that functors

\{-\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}({\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{q}},{A_{\mathfrak{q}}}_{\bullet})\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{q}\in\spec(A)\\
\mathrm{dim}(\mathfrak{q})\leq k\end{subarray}}

form a jointly consertative family on the subcategory
A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}-\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\subseteq\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
. Thus it suffices to prove that the map

(B.1.1.2)

\bigl(A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\longrightarrow\mathrel{{\mathop{\lim}\limits}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\bigr)\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}({\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{q}},{A_{\mathfrak{q}}}_{\bullet})

is an isomorphism for each such prime
\mathfrak{q}\subset A
. But a simple computation transforms (
B.1.1.2
) into

{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{q}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}{\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}A_{\mathfrak{q}}\;.

∎

Corollary B.1.2
.

The set of functors

\{(-)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\in\spec(A)\\
\mathrm{dim}(\mathfrak{p})\leq k\end{subarray}}

is jointly conservative on the full subcategory of
k
-complete modules. Moreover, the set of functors

\{-\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A/\mathfrak{p})_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{(A/\mathfrak{p})_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}\in\spec(A)\\
\mathrm{dim}(\mathfrak{p})\leq k\end{subarray}}

is jointly conservative on the full subcategory of
k
-complete modules.

Proof.

The first assertion follows immediately from Proposition
B.1.1
, the second one follows from the first one—see

[
10
,
Lemma 0G1U
]
.
∎

B.1.3.

We now prove that the
k
-completion is compatible with box restriction to a closed subscheme. This result will help us run the induction
during the proof of Proposition
B.2.1
, which is the main result of this appendix.
Let us first fix the following notation. If
B
is another ring of finite type, then to avoid confusion, we will, at certain times,
denote the functor of
k
-completion with respect to
B
by
(-)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{B,k}
.

Proposition B.1.4
.

For an
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
-module
M
and an ideal
I\subset A
, the canonical map

M^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{A,k}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A/I)_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}\;\longrightarrow\;(M\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A/I)_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}})^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{A/I,k}

is an isomorphism.

For the reader’s convenience, we will break the proof of
B.1.4

into several parts; the first part is a similar statement for a single completion applied to the ring.

Lemma B.1.5
.

For an ideal
I\subset A
and a closed subset
Y\subset\spec(A)
, the canonical map

A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A/I)_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}\;\longrightarrow\;(A/I)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y\cap\spec(A/I)}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Let us pick a set of generators
(f_{1},\ldots,f_{m})
for the ideal of
Y\subset\spec(A)
. Let
K_{n}
denote the Koszul complex on the sequence

f_{1}^{n},\ldots,f^{n}_{m}
. Now since the functor
-\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A/I)_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
distributes t-bounded products of compact objects, we can write
