\begin{split}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A/I)_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}&\;\simeq\;(\lim_{n}K_{n})\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A/I)_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;\lim_{n}K_{n}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A/I)_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}\;\simeq\;\lim_{n}\overline{K}_{n}\;\simeq\;(A/I)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y\cap\spec(A/I)}\;,\end{split}

where
\overline{K}_{n}\simeq K_{n}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A/I)_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
is the Koszul complex over
A/I
on the images of
f_{1}^{n},\ldots,f_{m}^{n}
under the factor map.
∎

The second step is to prove
B.1.4
for
M=A
.

Lemma B.1.6
.

For an ideal
I\subset A
, the canonical map

A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{A,k}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A/I)_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}\;\longrightarrow\;(A/I)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{A/I,k}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The fact that the functor
-\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A/I)_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
distributes t-bounded products of compacts together with
B.1.5

imply

A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{A,k}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A/I)_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}\;\simeq\;\bigl(\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y\subseteq\spec(A)\\
\mathrm{dim}(Y)\leq k\end{subarray}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y}\bigr)\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A/I)_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}\;\simeq\;\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y\subseteq\spec(A)\\
\mathrm{dim}(Y)\leq k\end{subarray}}(A/I)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y\cap\spec(A/I)}\;.

Therefore, we are left to show that the canonical map

\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y\subseteq\spec(A)\\
\mathrm{dim}(Y)\leq k\end{subarray}}(A/I)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Y\cap\spec(A/I)}\;\longrightarrow\;\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z\subseteq\spec(A/I)\\
\mathrm{dim}(Z)\leq k\end{subarray}}(A/I)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{Z}

is an isomorphism. By Proposition
2.2.2
applied to
A/I
, it suffices to prove that the map becomes an isomorphism
after the base change to
\bigl({\mathrel{{\mathop{\text{\phantom{a}}}\limits}}\!\!}\>(A/I)_{\mathfrak{q}},{(A/I)_{\mathfrak{q}}}_{\bullet}\bigr)
for each prime
\mathfrak{q}\in\spec(A/I)
of dimension not greater than
k
,
which it obviously does.
∎

We are now ready to finish the proof of Proposition
B.1.4
.

Proof of
B.1.4
.

Let
\omega_{1}
denote the smallest uncountable cardinal. The functor of
k
-completion can be written as

\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\Gamma_{k},-)\;,

where
\Gamma_{k}\;\simeq\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\Gamma_{Y}
can be written as a countable filtered colimit of
perfect
A
-modules; thus, the object
L_{k}
is
\omega_{1}
-compact, and the functor of
k
-completion preserves
\omega_{1}
-filtered limits. Moreover,
because the category
\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
is compactly generated by products of copies of
A
, it is also
\omega_{1}
-compactly generated by objects of the form

\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}\prod_{T}A
, and it therefore suffices to prove the claim for
M
having such form.

By reflexivity, Lemma
B.1.6
implies that the map

\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\Gamma_{Z}\;\longrightarrow\;\Gamma_{k}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A/I)_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}

is an isomorphism, so we are left to show that the map

\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\Gamma_{k},\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}\prod_{T}A)\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A/I)_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}\;\longrightarrow\;\uhom_{(A/I)_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\Gamma_{k}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A/I)_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}},\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}\prod_{T}A/I)\;.

By
[
10
,
Lemma 0DXC
]
, the object

\Gamma_{k}\;\simeq\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\Gamma_{Y}

has a finite Tor-amplitude. Thus, by Proposition
1.1.2
,
\Gamma_{k}
is quasi-isomorphic to a finite complex of free
A
-modules. So
it suffices to show that the map

\bigl(\prod_{\mathbb{N}}\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}\prod_{T}A\bigr)\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A/I)_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}\;\longrightarrow\;\prod_{\mathbb{N}}\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}\prod_{T}A/I

is an isomorphism, which follows immideately from Lemma
1.1.8
.
∎

B.2.

The main result of this appendix is the following statement, which says that the functor of
k
-completion is symmetric monoidal. In particular,
it follows from this statement, that the solid tensor product of a pair of
k
-complete modules is
k
-complete, which is a
k
-completion analog of
Proposition A.17
[
13
]
. We remark that we drop the boundedness assumption of loc.cit., but on the other hand, we impose a finiteness assumption on
A
.
For the sake of completeness, we also deduce in
B.2.9

the corresponding result for the functor of completion at a single ideal.

Proposition B.2.1
.

The functor of
k
-completion (which is a priori lax symmetric monoidal) is symmetric monoidal, i.e. for any pair
M,N\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
, the map

(B.2.1.1)

M^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}N^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\;\longrightarrow\;\bigl(M\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}N\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}

is an isomorphism. In particular, the tensor product of a pair of
k
-complete
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
-modules is
k
-complete.

B.2.2.

The proof of
B.2.1
occupies the rest of this appendix. First, we argue that it suffices to prove the proposition in the case
where the ring
A
is integral. Indeed, if
d
denotes the dimension of
A
, then the functor of
d
-completion is the identity functor. In particular,
both the source and the target of
B.2.1.1
are
d
-complete. We can now reduce to the integral case by Corollary

B.1.2
and Proposition
B.1.4
. From now on we assume that the ring
A
is integral.

By the same reduction as in
[
13
]
, it suffices to prove the claim for
M=\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}\prod_{T_{1}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}

and
N=\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}\prod_{T_{2}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}
. Namely, let
\omega_{1}
denote the smallest uncountable cardinal. On the one hand,
the functor of
k
-completion preserves
\omega_{1}
-filtered colimits (since it is given by internal Hom out of the fibre of a map between countable colimits of perfect modules), and on the other hand, the category
\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}
is generated under
\omega_{1}
-filtered colimits
by objects of the form
\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}\prod_{T}A
.

In addition, because

\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}\prod_{T}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\;\simeq\;\uhom_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(\Gamma_{k},\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}\prod_{T}A)\;,

and the object

\Gamma_{k}\;\simeq\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\Gamma_{Y}

is discrete and has finite Tor-amplitude (see
[
10
,
Lemma 0DXC
]
) and therefore
is quasi-isomorphic to a finite complex of free
A
-modules (see
1.1.2
), it follows from Lemma
1.1.8
that

\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}\prod_{T}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\;\simeq\;\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\prod_{T}A\;,

and it therefore suffices to prove the statement for
M=N=\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}
.

Summing up, the proof reduces to the following statement.

Lemma B.2.3
.

The canonical map

\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\;\longrightarrow\;\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}

is an isomorphism.

B.2.4.

Let
d
denote the dimension of
A
.
We first prove
B.2.3
in the case where
k=d-1
. Let
S=A\setminus 0\subset A

denote the multiplicative subset
of non-zerodivisors. We recall from
2.1.6
that the map

A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}\;\longrightarrow\;\lim_{f\in S}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{f}

is an isomoprhism.
Since we are working under the finite type hypothesis, the set
S
is countable. Let us fix an enumeration

\textstyle{\mathbb{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{S}
\textstyle{n\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\in$}}}
\textstyle{f_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\;,}
\scriptstyle{\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\in$}}}

and let us denote by
\widetilde{F}_{n}
the product
f_{1}f_{2}\ldots f_{n}\in S
. We get a string of isomorphisms

\begin{split}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}&\;\simeq\;\lim_{f\in S}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{f}\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;\lim_{n}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{\widetilde{F}_{n}}\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;\lim_{n}\lim_{m}A/\widetilde{F}_{n}^{m}\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;\lim_{n}A/\widetilde{F}_{n}^{n}\;.\end{split}

This allows us to write down the following colimit formula for
(\oplus_{\mathbb{N}}A)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}
.

Lemma B.2.5
.

The natural map

\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\mathrm{fib}\bigl(\prod_{n}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}\;\longrightarrow\;\prod_{n}A/F_{\phi(n)}\bigr)\;\longrightarrow\;\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}\;,

where, for
m\geq 1
,
F_{m}
denotes
\widetilde{F}_{m}
raised to the power of
m
, and
F_{0}
denotes
1\in A
, is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We present the completed direct sum as a fiber

\mathrm{fib}\bigl(\prod_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}A/F_{m}\longrightarrow\prod_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}A/F_{m}\bigr)\;.

Then, because there is no
lim^{1}
by M-L, this fiber is actually a kernel

(B.2.5.1)

\ker\bigl(\prod_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}A/F_{m}\longrightarrow\prod_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}A/F_{m}\bigr)\;.

We now rewrite the source of (
B.2.5.1
) as

\begin{split}\prod_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}A/F_{m}\;\simeq\;\prod_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\bigoplus_{S}A/F_{m}&\;\simeq\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\prod_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\bigoplus_{S_{m}}A/F_{m}\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\prod_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\bigoplus_{S_{m}}A/F_{m}\;,\end{split}

where the second isomorphism uses AB6 and the last isomorphism results from a cofinality argument. For a fixed collection
S_{1}\subseteq S_{2}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq\mathbb{N}
of nested finite subsets, we
construct a function
\phi\colon\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}
having value
m-1
on
S_{m}\setminus S_{m-1}
.
We observe that this gives us an isomorphism of diagrams

\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\{\text{finite }S_{m}\subset\mathbb{N}\}_{m}\\
S_{m}\subseteq S_{m+1},|S_{m}|\longrightarrow\infty\end{subarray}\right)\;\simeq\;\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\phi\colon\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}\\
\phi(n)\longrightarrow\infty\end{subarray}\right)\;,

and it remains to prove that, for a fixed such collection of subsets
\{S_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
and the corresponding function

\phi\colon\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
, we get an identification

(B.2.5.2)

\ker\bigl(\prod_{n\in\mathbb{N}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}\longrightarrow\prod_{n\in\mathbb{N}}A/F_{\phi(n)}\bigr)\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\;\ker\bigl(\prod_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\bigoplus_{S_{m}}A/F_{m}\longrightarrow\prod_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}A/F_{m}\bigr)\;.

We prove that both sides of (
B.2.5.2
) identify as submodules of
\prod_{\mathbb{N}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}
. The embedding of the right-hand side
into the product composed with the projection onto the
n
-th factor

\prod_{\mathbb{N}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}\;\longrightarrow\;A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}\;\simeq\;\ker\bigl(\prod_{m\in\mathbb{N}}A/F_{m}\longrightarrow\prod_{m\in\mathbb{N}}A/F_{m}\bigr)

is given by projections onto the
n
-th factor

\bigoplus_{S_{m}}A/F_{m}\;\hookrightarrow\;\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}A/F_{m}\;\longrightarrow\;A/F_{m}\quad\text{and}\quad\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}A/F_{m}\;\longrightarrow\;A/F_{m}\;;

in particular, we see that a tuple

(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},\ldots)\in\prod_{\mathbb{N}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}

is in the image if and only if, for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
n\in S_{m}\setminus S_{m-1}
, the element
a_{n}\in A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}
vanishes modulo
F_{m-1}
.
∎

By a similar argument, we get the following variant of the same statement.

Lemma B.2.6
.

The natural map

\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\mathrm{fib}\bigl(\prod_{n,m}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}\;\longrightarrow\;\prod_{n,m}A/(F_{\phi(n)}F_{\psi(m)})\bigr)\;\longrightarrow\;\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

For the completed direct sum, we write

\begin{split}\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}&\;\simeq\;\lim_{n}\;\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}A/F^{2}_{n}\bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;\lim_{n,m}\;\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}A/(F_{n}F_{m})\bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\;\simeq\;\ker\bigl(\prod_{n,m}\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}A/(F_{n}F_{m})\longrightarrow\prod_{n,m}\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}A/(F_{n}F_{m})\bigr)\;,\end{split}

and now, by an identical argument, we get

\begin{split}\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}\;\simeq\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\mathrm{fib}\bigl(\prod_{n,m}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}\;\longrightarrow\;\prod_{n,m}A/(F_{\theta_{1}(n)}F_{\theta_{2}(m)})\bigr)\;.\end{split}

It remains to observe that the subdiagram

\left(\begin{subarray}{c}(\phi,\psi)\colon\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\})\times(\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\})\\
\phi(n),\psi(m)\longrightarrow\infty\end{subarray}\right)\subseteq\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\theta\colon\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\})\times(\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\})\\
\theta(n,m)\longrightarrow\infty\end{subarray}\right)

is cofinal. Indeed, since both digrams are filtered, it suffices, for each
\theta
,
to construct a function of the form
(\phi,\psi)
satisfying
(\phi(n),\psi(m))\leq\theta(n,m)
for all
n,m
. One easily observes that
setting

(\phi(l),\psi(l))\;=\;(\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\limits}}\theta_{1}(n,m),\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{min}}\limits}}\theta_{2}(n,m))

does the job.
∎

Putting the last two statements together, we get
B.2.3
in codimension
1
.

Lemma B.2.7
.

The statement of
B.2.3
holds for
k=d-1
.

Proof.

\begin{split}&\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}\;\simeq\;\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\Bigl(\prod_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\prod_{m\in\mathbb{N}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}\longrightarrow\prod_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\prod_{m\in\mathbb{N}}A/F_{\phi(n)}\times A/F_{\psi(m)}\longrightarrow\prod_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\prod_{m\in\mathbb{N}}A/F_{\phi(n)}\otimes_{A}A/F_{\psi(m)}\Bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\;\mathrel{{\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}\limits}}\Bigl(\prod_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\prod_{m\in\mathbb{N}}A^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}\longrightarrow\prod_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\prod_{m\in\mathbb{N}}A/(F_{\phi(n)}F_{\psi(m)})\Bigr)\;\simeq\\
&\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{d-1}\;,\end{split}

where to get from the 2nd row to the 3rd we use the pull-back square

\textstyle{A/(F_{\phi(n)}F_{\psi(m)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A/F_{\phi(n)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A/F_{\psi(m)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A/F_{\phi(n)}\otimes_{A}A/F_{\psi(m)}\;.}

∎

B.2.8.

We will now finish the proof of
B.2.3
by the induction on dimension of
A
.
First, we note that the base cases of dimension
0
and
1
are, respectively, vacuous and already dealt with by
Lemma
B.2.7
. Let us now assume that the statement holds for any integral
A
of dimension up to
d>1

and all
k\in\{0,\dots,d\}
. We will now prove that the map

(B.2.8.1)

\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}\;\longrightarrow\;\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{k}

is an isomorphism for some fixed integral ring
A
of dimension
d+1
and some
k\in\{0,\dots,d\}
.
By Lemma
4.1.1
and Lemma
B.2.7
, both sides of
(
B.2.8.1
) are
d
-complete. By Corollary
B.1.2
,
it suffices to prove that the map
(\ref{eq-the-map-proof-tensor-product-of-k-complete-is-k-complete})\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A/\mathfrak{p})_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
is an isomorphism
for each prime
\mathfrak{p}\subset A
of dimension up to
d
, but this follows
from Lemma
B.1.4
and the induction hypothesis. It remains to note that the case
k=d+1
is vacuous. Lemma

B.2.3
and Proposition
B.2.1
are now proved.

Corollary B.2.9
.

The functor

(-)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I}\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\;\longrightarrow\;\mathrm{Mod}_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}

of completion at an ideal
I\subset A
is symmetric monoidal. In particular, the tensor product of a pair of
I
-complete
A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
-modules is
I
-complete.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that the map

\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I}\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I}\;\longrightarrow\;\bigl(\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}A\bigr)^{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$}}}}}_{I}

is an isomorphism.
Let
k
denote the dimension of
\spec(A/I)
. By Lemma
4.1.1
and Corollary
B.2.1

both source and target of the map are
k
-complete, so it suffices to prove that the map
becomes an isomorphism upon application of
-\otimes_{A_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}}(A/\mathfrak{p})_{\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\blacksquare$}}}}}
for each prime
\mathfrak{p}
of dimension up to
k
, which is clear.
∎
